Before that, **P1 was cleared**: the three items the 2026-07-28 analysis filed are fixed — `taskhub status` now probes services instead of ports and can say `WARN` when it doesn't know, dark is actually the default rather than only documented as one, and the closed mobile drawer is out of the tab order below `md`. Before that: **three silent-drift bugs found from live use**, all fixed 2026-07-25: a renamed category could take its whole folder out of every sync; `ensureCatalogOwner` repeated #19's mutable-email upsert key and had frozen the template catalog since login shipped; and a **stale container Prisma client made the MISSING feature a no-op that still reported success** — `TaskStatus.MISSING` was `undefined` and Prisma drops undefined fields. Plus the active/all filter toggle now shows which state it's in and how many tasks it's hiding. The narrative in this paragraph is the 2026-07-13 snapshot, kept as written. (Architecture direction decided: the template catalog becomes a **decoupled registry** the app fetches at runtime — versioned, **target-agnostic JSON** (not XML) on the Trigger → Action → Execution Target model, **compiled per target** (Windows real today, others honest-manual), served from a **static JSON registry first** (git repo + CDN) with a bundled fallback, API/admin later; see the P2 Template-registry item + the new Open decision. Also logged three new P2 items from dogfooding: **export existing tasks** (Windows→XML / native→JSON), the **PowerShell-console flash** from the self-heal tasks, and the **empty `\TaskHub\` folder + folder-naming** cleanup. Prior state: P1 QA is complete; P2 is rolling. The **Windows management pass is complete** — all three slices shipped: enable/disable, **schedule editing** (`task:update_schedule`), and now **action/settings editing** (`task:update` — command + working dir + description + run level, no-shell structured action, triggers/principal preserved, live-dogfooded against real Task Scheduler). Templates tab — **view selector (Grid/List/Kanban)**, **Resources menu**, and the **honesty pass** all shipped, closing "Template library UI" + "Cross-platform targets are cosmetic". **Favorite templates** also shipped (per-user star/unstar + Favorites filter, `TemplateFavorite` join, optimistic UI). **Template import/export**, **Save task as template**, and two dogfood fixes also shipped (2026-07-13): import/export (`denormalize.ts` + `GET /api/templates/export` / `POST /api/templates/import`) and Save-as-template (`templateFromTask.ts` + `POST /api/tasks/:id/save-as-template` → the same import pipeline) are the two non-reseed ways users/agents grow the catalog; the self-heal tasks no longer flash a PowerShell window (`run-hidden.vbs` + dedup) and the empty `\TaskHub\` scheduler folder is auto-pruned on last-task delete. **User resources & onboarding** also shipped (Help Center Getting Started walkthrough + Guides section + first-run banner), and **Export existing tasks** shipped (2026-07-13): per-task Export in the modal — Windows → native Task Scheduler XML via a read-only `task:export` agent round-trip (delivered UTF-16 LE + BOM so it re-imports cleanly), TaskHub-native → JSON from the DB. The **template tags model** also shipped (2026-07-13): free-form `tags String[]` on `Template` (distinct from the single `category`), carried through registry→DB→export, surfaced as a dedicated Tags facet + per-card chips (the old mislabeled "Tags" row is now "Category"). The **Developer Pack** also shipped (2026-07-13): 9 dev-workflow templates (git hygiene, npm dependency-check/build/test, .NET build, Docker prune + compose self-heal) in the bundled catalog + rebuilt registry (33 templates), all tagged via the tags model, plus a whole-catalog `commandTemplate` resolvability test. The **AI Pack (Claude Code)** also shipped (2026-07-13): 4 `ai-agent` templates that run the Claude Code CLI unattended as real creatable Windows tasks — headless `claude -p`, fenced by `--permission-mode dontAsk`, user-scoped `--allowedTools`, `--bare`, captured output (invocation verified vs. the current CLI docs); registry rebuilt 33 → **37** and **published** to the hosted repo (`https://mikesailab.com/taskhub-registry` verified serving all 37). The **Codex** slice also shipped and published: 3 `ai-agent` templates using `codex --ask-for-approval never exec`, explicit sandboxing, `--ephemeral`, final-message capture, and log capture; registry rebuilt 37 -> **40** and hosted index verified serving all 40. Latest verification: backend 192 unit + backend build for the Codex slice, prior baseline 74 integration, agent 71, frontend 55 + 7 E2E + lint/build. Next recommended work: continue the remaining P2/P3 follow-ups.)*

*2026-07-31 addendum: **Four Go-public checklist items shipped together — the launch blocker among them was a route nobody wanted.** (1) **`POST /auth/register` is deleted.** It was mounted, unauthenticated, on a service whose job is creating and running commands on your machine, so anyone who could reach `:3000` could mint themselves a real account and a valid token — and the final P3 item is specifically about making that service reachable from other devices. Deleted rather than flag-gated (a dev-only flag is one misconfiguration away from open). **Why it survived is the reusable part: the integration suite drove it for its own convenience, so the route had a constituency that wasn't a user.** Those tests now run through `/setup`, and one **pins the 404** as a tripwire. (2) **REST CORS now reads the same `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` list Socket.IO already enforced** — `createApp()` was a bare `app.use(cors())`, reflect-any-origin, while the socket channel was restricted: two mechanisms answering one question, which is how one of them quietly stops matching the other. Both now share one parser. Two calls the logged item didn't settle: a request with **no `Origin` header is always allowed** (every non-browser caller — MCP server, `curl`, the suite — and CORS was never their gate), and the item's **loopback condition was dropped as unobservable** (there is no bind config to read; the container must bind every interface) in favor of *empty list stays permissive **and warns at boot***, since "unset" and "deliberately open" are indistinguishable from inside the process and only one is a decision. (3) **Both package manifests said ISC** while the `LICENSE` file, the README badge and each other said Apache-2.0 — two machine-readable license claims contradicting the repo. Fixed; `version` fields deliberately left for *Versioning & releases*. (4) **The `check_db` pokes became `npm run db:check`**, kept rather than deleted because "what does the DB actually contain?" is the question that diagnosed [#22](troubleshooting/README.md#22-deleted-a-windows-task-synced-and-cronsole-still-shows-it--while-reporting-missing-n) — so it leads with the stale-generated-client check that makes every count under it trustworthy or not. **The malformed `.ts` one survived because `backend/tsconfig.json` includes only `src/`**: a `.ts` file in the package root is never typechecked, never built, never run, and can stay broken indefinitely with nothing to say so. **A `/sync-surfaces` pass over the diff then found four more stale claims, none of which any test could have caught:** CLAUDE.md §3 still advertised AWS/Render production hosting (retired by the 2026-07-13 local-first decision); `SECURITY.md` **and the Remote Access Guide** still described auth as "a development token, not a real login screen" — login shipped 2026-07-16, and **a security guide that is visibly out of date is one people stop believing**, so it now names what exists *and* what still doesn't, keeping the network gate as the real boundary; the skill's route table claimed "JWT access + refresh; rotation supported" when there is no refresh flow; and the integration matrix had CORS as untested (**I6.3**) while listing "**all 5 MCP tools**" (**I6.4**) against a surface of 15. Two more found the same way: the manual CORS check printed only `%{http_code}` — **but a refused origin still returns 200, so the check could not fail** (the [#25](troubleshooting/README.md#25-npx-tsc---noemit-in-frontend-passes-while-cis-build-fails-on-a-type-error) family, in a runbook this time) — and a **third** manifest, `agent/test-server/package.json`, also said ISC. The scripts README listed a deleted publish script. **Verified live** against the running backend, not just in tests: `/api/auth/register` → 404 with `needsSetup` unchanged, the allowed origin gets its `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, `http://evil.test` gets none, and a no-`Origin` authed call still returns 200. Tests: backend 417 unit (+11, mutation-checked) + 120 integration against real Postgres. See CHANGELOG › Security/Changed.*

*2026-07-28 addendum (5): **Run history export + the automation health score shipped**, together, because three separate logged items all needed the same **bulk `ExecutionLog` query** first. **The finding that reshaped the score came before any code:** `ExecutionLog` records runs **TaskHub performed**, not runs that happened — a Windows task firing on its own schedule writes nothing, and a manual Windows run's `SUCCESS` means "the agent started it". Scoring from it would have called nearly every task on a real machine never-run, at dashboard scale. So the evidence now comes from **Windows itself** (`lastTaskResult`, `numberOfMissedRuns`, added to the read-only `task:list`; **needs an agent republish**). Three rules hold the scorer honest: score only what can be evidenced, **absence of evidence is `unknown` and never `ok`**, and the number ranks rather than grades — it never appears without its signals. The CSV names what it contains, ships UTF-8 + BOM, and **neutralizes formula injection**, because TaskHub stores command lines and a task named `=cmd|'/c calc'!A1` would otherwise make the report a live payload. **Two design bugs were found by running it on the real 354-task machine and by no test**: system tasks dominated the "needs attention" list — re-creating the very burial the Personal lens shipped that morning to prevent — and `DBG_TERMINATE_PROCESS` was being read as a failing exit code. Both fixed and pinned. New traps [#29](troubleshooting/README.md#29-prisma-migrate-refuses-to-run-migration-was-modified-after-it-was-applied--and-offers-to-drop-your-database) (an applied migration file is immutable, comments included — and Prisma's only offered remedy drops the database) and [#30](troubleshooting/README.md#30-a-route-500s-on-real-data-while-tsc-is-green--a-cast-on-a-query-result) (a cast on a query result is a promise the query cannot keep). See CHANGELOG › Added.*

*2026-07-28 addendum (4): **Restore shipped — the backup story finally closes.** Bulk export landed earlier the same day and restore did not, which left the Tools tab able to tell you 56 tasks were gone and hand you a folder of XML, and unable to put one back. Delivered as a new **signed** agent verb (`task:import`), `POST /api/tools/restore/tasks`, and a Restore card on the Tools tab. **The design point that mattered most was not in the logged item:** the route always computes a **plan** first — create / overwrite / skip / refuse per file — from the machine's real tasks and folders, and both of those are readable through **read-only** verbs that already shipped. So `dryRun` is a genuine preview rather than a rehearsal, the count arrives before the click, and the preview works even against an agent that has not been republished. Three decisions carried through as designed: overwrite **refused by default** (an existing task reports the third state `exists` — not a failure, not a success — enforced by Windows' own `TaskCreation.Create`, not merely by our check); the **XML signed by sha256** because the XML *is* the task, its action, its trigger, and the account it runs as; and a deliberate, opt-in, always-reported **carve-out** to "TaskHub creates exactly one folder", since a restore is the user asking for their own tree back by name rather than TaskHub littering folders nobody asked for. **Verified live after an agent republish, reading Windows rather than TaskHub**: a probe task round-tripped with its principal intact (`UserId=mikes LogonType=Interactive RunLevel=Limited` — settling the one thing unit tests could not, that `TaskLogonType.None` preserves the XML's own principal), a second restore returned `exists` with the registration date **unchanged**, overwrite returned `replaced`, and a real `\Microsoft\Windows\Defrag\` export was refused with Defrag untouched. **The live pass also found the real cost of the folder carve-out** ([#28](troubleshooting/README.md#28-a-restored-task-or-the-folder-it-landed-in-cant-be-deleted-access-is-denied)): the agent is elevated, so a task it restores and any folder it creates carry an administrator ACE — the user who created the original unelevated **could not delete the restored copy**, and the empty folder needed an elevated prompt. The invariant's original rationale called a created folder "a one-way door only the user can close"; it is in fact a door **only an administrator** can close. The design stands, and the UI now says so before the click. Coverage: backend 361 unit + 8 integration against real Postgres, agent 151, frontend 145; four mutation checks. New traps: [#27](troubleshooting/README.md#27-a-route-that-takes-an-upload-413s--and-raising-the-global-body-limit-is-the-wrong-fix), [#28](troubleshooting/README.md#28-a-restored-task-or-the-folder-it-landed-in-cant-be-deleted-access-is-denied). See CHANGELOG › Added.*

*2026-07-28 addendum (3): **P2's two highest-value items shipped together, because they are one problem.** (1) **Untrack** — `DELETE /api/tasks/:id` removes the **real Task Scheduler entry**, and it was the only removal a user had, so an over-import (one click from the Import modal's defaults) had no undo that didn't destroy someone's actual scheduled tasks. `POST /api/tasks/:id/untrack` drops TaskHub's row and makes **no platform call**. The design turned out to live almost entirely in the **labeling** — two removals differing only in blast radius, one irreversible, get separate verbs, separate mutations, separate colours, and confirm copy naming **what survives**. The open decision resolved **yes** to an exclusion memory (`TaskExclusion`, keyed on the native `externalId`, **subtractive only** so the include-set keeps one definition), with the way back — an explicit category import — announced **before** the click (`excludedCount` in `/discover`) and after it (the sync toast). (2) **The system/personal split**, its other half: filtering hides them from view, untracking gets them out of the counts. A server-owned `isSystem` verdict plus a persisted **Personal** toggle that names what it hides. (3) **`untrack_task` on MCP (14 → 15 tools), ungated** — the sharper find of the day: the only removal an agent had was the gated, irreversible `delete_task`, so "clean up my dashboard" had exactly one tool and it destroyed real tasks. **Gating deletion is only honest once a safe removal exists without the gate** — the same reasoning that keeps `set_task_status` ungated. Verified live from **outside** TaskHub: after untracking, Windows' own `Get-ScheduledTask` still reported the task `Ready`; a tracked sync left it out; an explicit import brought it back. Tests: backend 328 + 6 new integration against real Postgres, frontend 138, MCP 135. New trap logged: [#26](troubleshooting/README.md#26-prisma-generate-fails-with-eperm-operation-not-permitted-rename--query_engine-windowsdllnode) — `prisma generate` fails `EPERM` while the backend holds the query-engine DLL, leaving the **DB ahead of the client**, which is #22's drift with a louder failure. See CHANGELOG › Added.*

*2026-07-28 addendum (2): **P1 is clear again — all three items from the outside analysis are fixed.** They shared a shape worth naming: **none of them was a broken feature, and all three were the product or its tooling saying something that wasn't true.** (1) **`taskhub status` called four services DOWN while all four served HTTP** — the same port check that once called a dead container's held port "backend already up" (#23), failing in the opposite direction because `catch { $false }` rendered *"the probe could not run"* as *"the service is down"*. Fixed by probing each service directly (`/api/health`, HTTP `GET /`, `pg_isready`, a RESP `PING` over the socket), demoting the port to corroboration, and adding a **third state**: present-but-unconfirmable is `WARN`, never a confident verdict, with the signal printed next to every row. **A control surface that is wrong in both directions is worse than no control surface, because it is consulted first and believed** ([troubleshooting #23a](troubleshooting/README.md#23a-and-the-same-probe-reported-four-services-down-while-all-four-were-serving)). (2) **Dark-by-default was documented in six places and implemented in none** — `readStored()` fell back to `'system'`, so a light machine got a light TaskHub while the README wore a "dark by default" badge; the code now matches the records, and the **Open decision is closed in favor of dark** because the tokens and the public site are built dark-first. (3) **The closed mobile drawer was hidden only visually** — `-translate-x-full` leaves the nav in the tab order and the a11y tree, so a keyboard user walked an invisible menu; it is now `inert` + `aria-hidden` below `md` only, since the same element is the real navigation above it. Verification notes kept honest on each item: the status script was proven in **both** directions (a simulated broken environment and a raw accept-but-never-answer socket) and parse-checked under PowerShell 5.1 as well as 7; the theme was confirmed through the toggle's `aria-pressed` rather than `<html class>`, which would not discriminate `dark` from `system` on a dark-preferring machine; and the drawer's `<375px` layout was **not** re-driven live because the browser window would not resize in this environment — what was verified live is that Chrome actually refuses focus once `inert` is applied. Frontend 124 tests + typecheck + lint green. See CHANGELOG › Fixed/Changed.*

*2026-07-28 addendum: **A second outside-eyes analysis landed, and its actionable content is folded into the items below.** An independent review pass (`.claude/temp/taskhub-analysis-7_28_26/taskhub-analysis-2026-07-28.md`, local-only) read the repo, drove the live app in a real browser against the **352-task** machine, and filed 14 findings, a UI/UX list, 3 tiers of suggested features, and 15 brand-new product directions. **Every code-level claim was re-verified against the tree before being written here** — the ones that held are now items with file:line evidence; the ones already tracked are cross-referenced rather than duplicated (dry-run/schedule tester, bulk enable/disable, restore-from-export, scheduled backups, run-history CSV, and the gallery import handoff were all already logged). One finding is **not ours**: the sandbox could not write to `Agents/Codex/.codex/temp`, which is a workspace ACL issue outside this repo. The pattern across the new items is the same one this project keeps re-learning: **the app is honest about what it does and quiet about what it doesn't** — a dashboard where 257 of 352 rows are `\Microsoft\` system tasks, a delete that can only mean "delete from Windows", a status script that reports four services down while all four serve HTTP, and a theme that documents itself as dark-by-default while defaulting to `system`. New items are grouped under P1 (honesty/correctness), P2 (density at real scale), P3 (the new product bets), and the Go-public checklist (metadata + surface cleanup).*

*2026-07-27 addendum (2): **The un-imported-tasks signal shipped** (P3) — the dashboard now says what a sync *didn't* import. See the ticked item under P3 and CHANGELOG › Added. The design decision the logged item didn't anticipate: **`\Microsoft\` tasks are counted separately from the headline number**, because on a real machine they are 257 of 352 and would pin the message at a constant — and a warning that never changes re-creates the invisibility it was meant to fix.*

*2026-07-27 addendum: **A local outage traced to a boot-order race, fixed structurally in two places** (infra; not a planned roadmap item, logged here because it changed how the stack starts). Reported as "TaskHub is giving me a network error": every container read `Up`, the UI loaded fine, and nothing served `:3000` for ~12 minutes. **Nothing waited for Postgres to be *ready*, only to *exist*** — after an unclean shutdown it spends seconds in crash recovery refusing connections, and the boot seed dies on the refusal. It bit **both** backends with different error strings (container: `FATAL: the database system is starting up`; host: `Can't reach database server at localhost:5432`). What made it permanent rather than transient is the supervisor: **`tsx watch` survives the crash**, so the container never exits and `restart: unless-stopped` never fires, while Docker's port proxy holds `:3000` with nothing behind it. **Compounding cause: two stacks were running at once** — this repo runs backend/frontend on the **host** (`scripts/taskhub.ps1`; Docker is db+redis only), but a stray full `docker compose up -d` had started rival containers; the container took `:3000`, the host backend then couldn't bind it, and once the container died the port was held by a corpse — while `taskhub.ps1`'s `Test-Port 3000` liveness check reported **"backend already up"** and refused to start the real one. Every layer reported something true and the sum was a lie. Fixed with a `pg_isready` healthcheck + `condition: service_healthy` (container), a new `Wait-Db` in `taskhub.ps1` (host), and **`profiles: ["docker"]` on backend/frontend** so a plain `docker compose up -d` is db+redis only and cannot create a rival stack (`docker compose --profile docker up -d` for the containerized variant). The frontend also moved off Vite's default `5173` → **`7373`** with `strictPort`, since the collision was between the repo's own two copies. Two generalizations now in the traps table: **a container whose command is a watcher sits `Up` after the app inside dies — "container is `Up`" is not "app is serving"**, and **never probe your own service with a bare port check; a bound port proves something holds it, not that it's yours.** [troubleshooting #23](troubleshooting/README.md#23-network-error-after-a-reboot--the-database-system-is-starting-up).*

Registry rebuilt and published **37 -> 40**; hosted index verified serving all 40 with the 3 new `ai-codex-*` entries.*

Added a 15-template Extended Pack (system/backup/cleanup/monitoring/data/notification), so the catalog is now **55 templates = 5 core (built-in) + 50 extended (gallery-only)** — the registry is genuinely larger than the default install. Download / Copy JSON feed the shipped Templates → Import path. *2026-07-15 addendum: **MCP verified end-to-end after a System Restore, and `convert_schedule` made honest in text.** A restore of `C:` wiped two pieces of per-machine state git cannot protect — the `\Task-Hub\TaskHubRepublish` registration and the `TASKHUB_TOKEN` User env var — while the repo on `D:` stayed clean, so the failures looked unrelated to their cause (logged as [troubleshooting #11](troubleshooting/README.md#11-after-a-system-restore-the-republish-task-and-mcp-tools-are-gone) + a skill trap). The 2026-07-14 hardening worked as designed: the server **refused to start** rather than forward a literal `${TASKHUB_TOKEN}`, so the tools went missing instead of 403-ing misleadingly. Re-registered + re-minted, then verified the MCP server over stdio as a real client: handshake, all 5 tools, `list_tasks` (73), `list_templates` (5 = the core tier, confirming prune-on-sync), and `convert_schedule` — the multi-day weekly fix confirmed still holding (`0 9 * * 1-5` → all five days). Shipped from it: **`convert_schedule` now renders the converted trigger in its text output**, because a bare "confidence 1" is exactly what the Monday-only bug printed; the fallback path (Feb 30 → a silently **hourly** trigger at confidence 0.7) is now visible too. Presentation only — the wrapper still owns no logic. See CHANGELOG › Changed.*

*2026-07-15 addendum: **The MCP surface is now fully verified live — and the first live run found a broken core template.** `create_task_from_template` and `run_task` had **never been fired live** (deliberately skipped at ship time to avoid registering real Windows tasks), leaving the two side-effecting tools — the half that touches the user's machine — unproven. Both now verified end-to-end against real Task Scheduler and cleaned up: create registers a **direct no-shell exec** (P0 guarantee confirmed on a real registration, not just in tests), the agent creates and later auto-prunes its own `\TaskHub` folder, `run_task` executes, and a bogus id returns an honest `HTTP 404`. The UTC→local trigger conversion was confirmed **correct** via a discriminating pair — `0 3 * * 1-5` → `20:00 Sun–Thu` (day rolls back) vs `0 18 * * 1-5` → `11:00 Mon–Fri` (same day); an off-by-one bug would have shifted both. **The find:** `tpl_starter_webhook_windows` (**core**, ships to every fresh install, `*/15` default) used `Invoke-WebRequest` without `-UseBasicParsing` — it NullRefs on Windows 11 (no IE engine) and **hangs forever** under Task Scheduler, stranding a `powershell.exe` every 15 minutes while TaskHub reports `SUCCESS`. Fixed in `bundled.ts` + registry rebuilt (55 templates, 230 backend tests green). **The lesson, now written into the authoring docs:** resolvability proves tokenization, **not** that a command runs on the target — a green suite is not evidence a template works. Logged as troubleshooting **#12**. **Published + verified live** — `https://mikesailab.com/taskhub-registry` serves the fixed command (55 templates, `index.json` sha256 matching the served bytes), so existing installs converge on their next sync with no redeploy. Also flipped `TEMPLATE_REGISTRY_URL` to default **unset**: `.env.example` shipped the public CDN as the default, so a local template edit was invisible until published *publicly* — you had to ship a broken template to the world to test its fix. A local-first app gets a local-first dev loop; the hosted URL is now opt-in.*

*2026-07-15 addendum: **`list_folders` shipped — `create_task`'s `folder` parameter is now answerable.** `create_task` went out with a `folder` an agent had **no honest way to fill**: TaskHub creates only `\TaskHub` and refuses a create into a missing folder, so every other folder must already exist — and nothing over MCP could discover which do. Wraps the existing read-only `GET /api/tasks/folders`; **sequenced first of the remaining surface-expansion tools precisely because it's read-only**, so it raises none of the destructive-op gating question the mutating verbs still wait on. It carries the route's two honesty behaviors through rather than re-deciding them in the wrapper: **unwritable folders are listed, not filtered** (an omission reads as *"that folder doesn't exist"* and sends the caller hunting for something it can already see), and **`defaultFolder` is reported separately** from the listing — because `\TaskHub` is created lazily and **pruned when its last task is deleted**, it is routinely *absent* from a real machine's list, and an agent reading absence as "unusable" would go invent a folder that then gets refused. **That second case was found by driving the tool live** — the listing came back without `\TaskHub` minutes after the day's test tasks were deleted — which is exactly the class of thing the stubbed suite is documented as unable to catch. The live run also re-confirmed [#9](troubleshooting/README.md#9-agent-payload-arrives-with-every-field-empty)'s camelCase fix **on the very route that produced that bug** (every field populated, not a well-formed payload of blanks). Tests: 12 new (**75** total), mutation-checked. Surface is now **7 tools**. See CHANGELOG › Added.*

*2026-07-15 addendum: **The MCP server has a test suite — and CI enforces it.** It shipped with none (tools covered only by the REST endpoints beneath them; CI build-only), so a broken tool surface could reach a host unchallenged. **63 tests**, wired into the CI `mcp-server` job. Scope follows the wrapper's contract — it owns **no logic**, so the suite pins exactly what it *does* own (registration, schema validation, filtering/limits, body shaping, error normalization, honest rendering) and leaves the backend to its own suites. `tools.test.ts` drives the **real tools** through a **real MCP client** over `InMemoryTransport` with a stubbed client, so a tool that fails to register or a schema that rejects valid input fails a test; `client.test.ts` runs against a **real local HTTP server** rather than a mocked axios, because the thing under test *is* how axios reports failures. Guards worth knowing: the surface test pins **exactly six tool names** (a deliberate tripwire — that change obligates both README tables + the skill per §11a); the `${TASKHUB_TOKEN}` literal guard is pinned **in both directions** (a false positive would be a refusal to start); and `convert_schedule` must spell out all five weekdays — the rendering is the only thing that made the Monday-only bug visible at confidence 1.0. **The suite was mutation-tested** (drop the weekday rendering / swallow the lossy warnings / put undefined optionals on the wire → each failed exactly one test, all green on restore), because a suite that can't fail is decoration — the same "green is not evidence" rule the catalog learned in [#12](troubleshooting/README.md#12-a-template-passes-every-test-and-still-hangs-on-the-target). **New honest gap logged** in `docs/testing/`: the suite stubs the client, so it proves what the wrapper does, not that the wrapper and the API still **agree** — [#9](troubleshooting/README.md#9-agent-payload-arrives-with-every-field-empty) one layer up. Hand-driving a wrapped route after changing it is still required. See CHANGELOG › Added.*

*2026-07-15 addendum: **`create_task` shipped — the MCP surface can now create a task from a command, not just a template.** Part (a) of *MCP surface expansion* (the headline gap) plus part (b) (the skill guide) landed together. `create_task` wraps the existing `POST /api/tasks` with **no new backend logic**, so every guarantee stays where it's tested; the surface is now **6 tools**. **Verified live over stdio** against the real backend + agent: registered as a **direct exec** (`Execute: powershell.exe` — the P0 no-shell guarantee on a real registration, not a test), trigger converted UTC→local correctly (`0 9 * * *` → `02:00 -07:00`), and ran to completion — proven by Windows' `LastTaskResult: 0` **plus a real side effect**, since TaskHub's own `SUCCESS` is what a hung task reports too. Test task deleted; `\TaskHub` auto-pruned. Shipped alongside it: `skills/cronsole/references/task-authoring.md` — the judgment half, because a create tool without the invariants (UTC cron, no-shell, `\Microsoft\` refused, folder-must-exist, `0.7` = replaced) just creates wrong tasks confidently. **Still open in this item:** the management verbs (enable/disable, re-schedule, edit, delete, history, export, list_folders) and the three open questions — destructive-op gating being the real one, since those routes mutate real Task Scheduler entries under an **elevated** agent and MCP has no equivalent of the UI's confirm dialog. **"The route already exists" is not on its own an argument for exposing it.** See CHANGELOG › Added.*

*2026-07-15 addendum: **All 5 MCP tools re-verified live from a real host, and the "safe" test schedule turned out to be the trap.** A second live pass (after the [#13](troubleshooting/README.md#13-the-cronsole-mcp-tools-are-missing-while-the-token-is-fine) disabled-server fix) exercised the full surface from Claude Code as a genuine MCP client: `list_tasks` (73), `list_templates` (**5** — the core tier, confirming prune-on-sync), `convert_schedule` honest in all three registers (`0 9 * * 1-5` → all five days at 1.0, a `*/7` step → 0.7 + lossy warning, garbage → 0 + reason), then `create_task_from_template` → a real `\TaskHub\` entry and `run_task` → `LastTaskResult: 0`. **The webhook hang fix ([#12](troubleshooting/README.md#12-a-template-passes-every-test-and-still-hangs-on-the-target)) is confirmed live** — the registered action carries `-UseBasicParsing` and Windows itself recorded a clean exit. Worth recording *how* that was confirmed: TaskHub's own `lastRunStatus: SUCCESS` is **not** evidence — it is precisely what the bug also printed — so the proof had to come from Windows' `LastTaskResult`, not from TaskHub. #12's lesson generalizes: **when the thing you're testing is the reporting layer's honesty, the reporting layer cannot be your witness.** **The find:** `0 4 1 1 *` was chosen as a can't-possibly-fire schedule for the throwaway task, and registered as **daily, repeating hourly** — the converter's hard-coded fallback. Logged as [troubleshooting #14](troubleshooting/README.md#14-a-rare-cron-becomes-an-hourly-trigger) + a skill trap, with two new P3 items below: **MCP surface expansion** (a free-form `create_task` + management tools — every one wrapping a route that **already exists**, paired with a skill section that teaches the invariants an agent would otherwise violate) and **naming the frequency in the fallback warning**. Docs/roadmap only — no code changed, so no CHANGELOG entry.*

*2026-07-25 addendum (3): **The active/all filter toggle now differentiates its two states, and admits what it's hiding.** Reported as "a little confusing": the states differed only by a `bg-primary/10` tint and the eye icon's slash, which at 10% opacity on a dark surface is no differentiation at all — and a single button labelled with a *state* ("Active Only") reads equally as an *action*. Now green tint + green `EyeOff` for Active Only (reusing the `ACTIVE`-dot green, so the association is already learned) vs amber tint + amber `Eye` for Showing All, plus `aria-pressed` and honest tooltips. **The load-bearing addition is the count, not the colour:** the filter keeps `status === 'ACTIVE'` only, so Active Only silently hid `MISSING` and `UNKNOWN` too — meaning throughout the very session that produced addendum (2), the 49 natively-deleted tasks were **invisible in the default view** with no hint they existed. The button now reads `Active Only · 110 hidden`; a dashboard that withholds 110 rows without saying so is lying by omission, which is the §9 register again in miniature. Colour + icon + count = three redundant signals, so nothing rests on colour alone. Verified in a real browser in both states (and that verification is what surfaced the MISSING-hidden-by-default problem — the code review alone did not). Frontend 81 tests + typecheck + lint. See CHANGELOG › Changed.*

*2026-07-25 addendum (2): **The MISSING-status feature had never once worked in the Docker stack — and reported success the whole time.** Found by a user deleting a whole `\Edge-Radar\` folder plus every task under `\AI-Automation-Library\`'s subfolders: syncing left all 50 listed as `ACTIVE`/`DISABLED` while the response read `{"count":349,"missing":56}`, i.e. **claimed** it had marked them. The DB was byte-identical before and after. Cause: the container's **generated Prisma client** predated the `MISSING` enum (shipped 2026-07-16 in this very roadmap), so `TaskStatus.MISSING` was `undefined` — and **Prisma treats `undefined` in a `data` payload as "leave this field alone"**, so the write degraded to `{ nextRunTime: null }` while `updateMany` still returned a matched count the route faithfully reported. **A wrong enum value throws; a missing one silently no-ops** — that asymmetry is the whole trap. The client was stale by the same mechanism as troubleshooting #18 (compose shadows `node_modules`, so a host `prisma generate` never reaches the container) while `prisma migrate` updated the **database** — so DB and client drifted apart with nothing to notice. Regenerated in-container and **guarded**: a loud boot banner (`warnOnStaleGeneratedClient`, non-fatal by design) plus `reconcileMissingTasks` **throwing instead of partial-writing**, so the route returns an honest per-platform error rather than a false number. Verified live (56 rows actually flipped: the user's 24 + 26, plus the 4 `SoftLanding` + 2 `TaskHub` strays flagged earlier the same day). **The lesson worth keeping:** nine days of green suites couldn't see this because they `vi.mock('@prisma/client')` — **the mock asserted the code's intent while the container disagreed with it**, so a bug living in the generated client was structurally invisible. Now pinned by a test that asserts the *real* generated client is current. Same family as #9 (stubbed serializer) and the MCP suite's stubbed HTTP client: **when the bug lives in the layer your suite replaces, drive the real path once.** Tests: backend 253 unit (5 new, mutation-tested) + typecheck. See CHANGELOG › Fixed and [troubleshooting #22](troubleshooting/README.md#22-deleted-a-windows-task-synced-and-cronsole-still-shows-it--while-reporting-missing-n).*

*2026-07-25 addendum: **Two silent-drift bugs, both found by a user asking "why isn't TaskHub bringing in my new tasks?" — and the answer to the original question was "nothing is broken."** The agent was online and had been reporting all 399 Windows tasks the whole time; the missing ones (`\IAM\`, `\Edge-Radar-MikesAILab\`) simply lived in folders never imported, and **Sync Now cannot discover a new folder** — it built its category filter from the tasks already on screen, so a new folder was excluded by the very filter meant to include it. That's working as designed (selective import) but silent, so it reads as a broken sync; logged as [troubleshooting #20](troubleshooting/README.md#20-sync-now-never-brings-in-a-task-you-just-created-in-task-scheduler) with the two facts that make it navigable — **there is no automatic Windows sync at all** (no poll, no interval; `NativeScheduler` is native jobs, `catalogSync` is templates), and `Microsoft`/`Uncategorized` are unticked by default and all-or-nothing per category. **(1) The real bug behind it (fixed):** categories are user-renameable while the sync filter matches on `extractCategory(externalId)` re-derived from the **folder path**, so sending stored category names meant a renamed category matched no folder and that folder silently stopped syncing *and* refreshing — a single-task folder went dark entirely. `POST /api/tasks/sync` now takes `{ scope: 'tracked' }` (mutually exclusive with `categories`) and resolves the include-set server-side from tracked tasks' native paths (`TaskService.trackedCategories`), keeping one definition of "category" on the server; unit- + mutation-tested, and live-verified by renaming a single-task folder's category and confirming it still synced. **Rule: never filter or look up by a user-editable label.** **(2) Found incidentally while restarting the backend to verify (1):** `ensureCatalogOwner()` keyed its upsert on the **mutable** `CATALOG_OWNER_EMAIL` while creating the fixed `CATALOG_OWNER_ID` — the identical bug fixed in `src/index.ts` on 2026-07-16, **surviving in a second call site that fix didn't touch**. Because the caller *catches* it, the backend booted clean and the only symptom was a **Templates tab frozen since the day login shipped**; registry rebuilds had no effect. Fixed and verified by the success line, not by absence of the error. Two generalizable lessons: **a swallowed error on a background refresh is invisible in exactly the way a crash isn't**, and **when you fix a bug of this shape, grep for the pattern rather than the instance** (`grep -rn "upsert" backend/src | grep -v "where: { id"`). Still open, deliberately not done: the UI gives **no signal** that tasks exist in folders you haven't imported — an empty-diff Sync Now could hint "N tasks in un-imported folders", which would have prevented the whole investigation. Tests: backend 248 unit + typecheck, frontend 81 + typecheck, all green. See CHANGELOG › Fixed and troubleshooting [#20](troubleshooting/README.md#20-sync-now-never-brings-in-a-task-you-just-created-in-task-scheduler)/[#20a](troubleshooting/README.md#20a-and-a-renamed-category-silently-stops-syncing-its-folder)/[#21](troubleshooting/README.md#21-templates-never-update-catalog-sync-failed--p2002-on-every-boot).*

*2026-07-16 addendum (2): **Single-user local login shipped — the first Go-public *Application* item.** The dashboard authenticated with a hand-minted `VITE_DEV_TOKEN` and had no login UI; now there's a real first-run + login flow. Backend: `GET /auth/status`, `POST /auth/setup` (first-run, **claims** the password-less `cli_user_placeholder` the boot seed creates to own the catalog — the load-bearing find from live testing, since otherwise the setup screen never shows), `PATCH /auth/password`, and login rate-limiting. Frontend: an `AuthProvider`/`AuthGate` state machine (create-account → sign-in → app), a dynamic token store the axios interceptor + `/ui` socket both read, 401→auto-logout, logout, and change-password. `VITE_DEV_TOKEN` is now a dev/E2E fallback; the MCP `TASKHUB_TOKEN` is untouched. Verified end-to-end in a real browser (fresh DB → create → dashboard → logout → login, 0 console errors) plus backend integration against real Postgres. Multi-tenant (open registration, reset, refresh, per-user pairing) stays deferred. Tests: backend 244 unit + 14 auth integration (+3 rate-limiter), frontend 77. See CHANGELOG › Added and Go-public › Account/login system.*

*2026-07-16 addendum: **The three loose ends logged from the MCP/nonce work are all shipped** (P3, docs/roadmap + code). (1) **Reconcile vanished tasks → MISSING, not silent delete.** Sync reconciliation used to hard-delete any task absent from the platform snapshot; but absence isn't proof (an offline agent / an ACL'd folder is indistinguishable from a native delete), and the symptom was the opposite tail — two rows sitting `ACTIVE` + due while gone. `removeStaleTasks` → `reconcileMissingTasks`: mark `MISSING` (new `TaskStatus` + migration `20260716120000_add_task_status_missing`), clear `nextRunTime`, keep the row + logs; self-heals on the next sync that sees the task; explicit `DELETE` is the only removal. UI shows an amber MISSING dot + honest "not found, re-sync or delete" note; MCP `list_tasks` gains a MISSING filter, `set_task_status` refuses it. Verified against real Postgres 16. (2) **Split the `0.7` confidence score** with a `lossy: 'approximated' | 'replaced'` discriminator on `ConversionResult` (the score deliberately unchanged), surfaced through the preview/create routes and rendered distinctly by `convert_schedule` + `create_task`. (3) **Swept the repo for literal control bytes** — found + fixed NULs in `tools.test.ts` and `0x1f` in a C# test comment (both to escapes), added `scripts/check-control-bytes.mjs` + a CI `repo-hygiene` job, mutation-checked. Tests: backend 241 unit + 7 targeted integration (real Postgres), MCP 127, frontend 70 + lint/build — all green; `mcp-server/dist/` rebuilt. See CHANGELOG › Changed/Fixed.*

*2026-07-13 direction change: **TaskHub launches local-first — cloud/SaaS hosting is dropped as the default.** The old P3 "host the backend + frontend on a Hetzner VPS → hosted control plane" item is replaced by **Remote access (self-hosted)** — reaching your own local instance from other devices via Tailscale / Cloudflare Tunnel (the code-server model) — moved to the **bottom of P3 as the final, optional enhancement**; go-live does not depend on it. Guide drafted: `docs/user-guides/guides/Remote_Access_Guide.md`. The "Final hosting choice" open decision is resolved as moot, and the Go-public checklist now reflects that there is no hosted instance at launch.*

---

#### ✅ Completed

##### Foundation — MVP build-out (Phases 0–3, Jan–Jun 2026, archived)

- Discovery, requirements, architecture, and competitive research (archived locally under `docs/archive/`).
- **Windows Task Scheduler end-to-end**: .NET agent ↔ Socket.io backend ↔ Prisma/Postgres ↔ React dashboard; manual run trigger; enable/disable.
- **Dashboard UX**: dark theme, 4 view modes (grid/list/kanban/schedule), category chips with counts, selective import with local category overrides, task cloning, Help Center.
- **Template library**: two-tier catalog (20 script starters + use-case patterns), Apply modal with `{{placeholder}}` parameters and client+server validation.
- Auth scaffold (JWT), Docker Compose dev stack, GitHub Actions CI, unit test suites across backend (Vitest) / frontend (RTL) / agent (xUnit).

##### Reliability & product sprint (July 2026)

- **Schedule timezone — the app is authored in your zone (Pacific by default), the database is still UTC** (P2 product value; found by using the product): applying a template put a task on a UTC clock time. Nothing along the pipeline was wrong — `0 8 * * *` was stored as declared, converted to a `Daily @ 08:00` trigger, and registered by the agent at the correct **local** equivalent (`TriggerBuilder.UtcTimeToLocalToday`) — so no test could fail and no honesty rule was violated. The defect was only that **the one number a human reads and types was in a zone nobody thinks in**, and the conversion was homework. The fix is a conversion layer at the **edge**: `frontend/src/utils/timezone.ts` + `useScheduleZone`, with `Settings › Schedule timezone` holding a real IANA zone (default `America/Los_Angeles`; Machine local, UTC and ten common zones offered). Applied across the four authoring surfaces (Apply / New task / Edit schedule / Schedule tester), the preset chips, the template cards, `describeCron`, the dashboard clock readings, and the trigger start-boundary row.

  **The design decision that keeps it honest: the zone stops at the browser.** Storage, the REST API, the signed agent command and all 15 MCP tools stay UTC — a cron whose meaning depended on its author would fork every expression in the database, and `create_task` cannot see a browser preference. So the *machine* surface is unambiguous and only the *human* surface converts, with each field printing the stored UTC form beside it so the dashboard and `list_tasks` never appear to disagree.

  **Two things it refuses to guess.** A date-pinned expression whose conversion crosses midnight (`0 4 1 1 *` → 31 December in Pacific) cannot be written as cron, so it stays UTC and says so — the same instinct as the `approximated` schedules that deliberately get no dates. And **DST is stated, not hidden**: a Windows task holds its local wall-clock across a transition, while a Cronsole-native task shifts an hour, because `NativeScheduler` evaluates the stored UTC cron forever — an asymmetry the UI names on the field rather than leaving to be discovered in November. Day fields are expanded before rolling, and **only when the shift crosses midnight** (`0 22 * * 1-5` → `0 6 * * 2,3,4,5,6`; a same-day shift leaves `1-5` alone rather than churning the stored value) — the `parseInt('1-5')` trap that once made every weekday schedule Monday-only in three layers. Tests: **33 new** unit cases exercising both halves of the DST year, plus two Apply-modal tests pinning that the field pre-fills in-zone and the request carries UTC; frontend suite **219 green**, build clean. *(2026-07-31)*

- **Post-rename full test sweep — every automated suite run, and the four things it found** (2026-07-31): ran the whole of `docs/testing/` except the manual runbooks, against the renamed repo. **Everything green**: backend 457 unit + 132 integration (real Postgres), frontend 195 unit + lint + build, agent 151 xUnit, mcp-server 139 + typecheck + build, Playwright **9/9**, and both hygiene scripts (12 `.ps1` pure ASCII, 629 files free of control bytes). The value wasn't the green, though — it was the four failures **no suite was positioned to report**. **(1) The login screen still said "TaskHub"**, because the markup split it as `Task<span>Hub</span>` and a brand broken across an element is invisible to the grep that verifies a rename; the E2E suite authenticates past that screen with the dev token, so `AuthScreen` had never rendered under test. Fixed to one contiguous string and pinned by two `AuthFlow.test.tsx` cases on the rendered heading. **(2) The E2E suite could not run at all** — `VITE_DEV_TOKEN` was empty, so all nine specs died on the login screen looking like a broken frontend. Since E2E is the **only** suite not in CI, one empty credential disabled the entire full-stack gate while everything else stayed green ([#36](troubleshooting/README.md#36-every-playwright-e2e-test-fails-on-a-heading-that-exists--the-browser-is-sitting-on-the-login-screen)). **(3) Running E2E strands the real Windows agent offline** and it does not self-heal — [#5](troubleshooting/README.md#5-windows-offline-after-running-a-transient-test-agent) reached through the mock agent rather than a hand-started one, now [#37](troubleshooting/README.md#37-running-the-e2e-suite-knocks-your-real-windows-agent-offline--and-it-stays-that-way), with the health check written into the run procedure instead of left as folklore. **(4) The one open dependency advisory had a forward fix all along** — see the react-router entry in `CHANGELOG.md`. Plus three **false ✅ claims** in the testing docs themselves (a JWT refresh flow that has never existed, asserted twice; a login rate limit described as unimplemented that has shipped since 2026-07-16; a `taskhub.theme` storage key that is now `cronsole.theme`) and six `psql -U cronsole -d cronsole` commands in the manual runbooks that fail on the first step — **no such role exists**, the data layer deliberately stayed `taskhub`. **The pattern worth keeping:** every one of these lived in a *describing* surface — a doc, a credential, a brand string, an advisory note — which is precisely the class §11a exists for, because none of them can fail a test. A green sweep is evidence about the code and about nothing else.

- **Per-command nonce on signed agent commands — a legitimate re-send is no longer eaten by the replay guard** (P3; the fix for [troubleshooting #16](troubleshooting/README.md#16-a-second-identical-agent-command-within-one-second-is-dropped)): `ts` is second-granular and was the only varying field, so two identical commands inside one second produced a **byte-identical message and signature**. The agent's replay guard — correctly — cannot distinguish that from a captured frame, so it dropped the second one **silently**, and the backend, having no reply, timed out after 15s and reported `Agent trigger timeout`: the transport blamed for a clock-resolution problem. User-visible as a double-clicked **Run Now** hanging 15s. Fixed by putting a fresh 16-byte **nonce inside the signed message**, immediately before `ts`, in all six commands (`emitSignedCommand` generates it; `signCommand` takes it as a parameter exactly as `ts` does, keeping the golden vectors deterministic). **Inside** the signature, not merely alongside — an unsigned nonce could be rewritten in flight to turn a replayed frame into a "fresh" command, the same reasoning that already puts `folder` and `trigger` under the signature. **The replay cache needed no change**: keyed on `(ts, sig)`, it stops colliding on its own once the signature is unique per instance; the `*Message` helpers take the nonce as a **required** parameter, which is the enforcement that one always exists. No back-compat shim — backend and agent ship together by design, so an optional nonce would mean two valid message forms, weakening the guarantee to serve a skew the project doesn't support; a skewed agent fails closed. **Cross-language golden HMAC vectors regenerated**, and — per the established practice — the generator was first validated by **reproducing the previously-committed vectors from the old format**, because a generator that can't reproduce what's already in the repo emits new values that are confidently wrong and then get pinned by both suites (a lie agreed on twice). Also updated `agent/test-server/index.js`, which signs commands to a real agent and would otherwise have been silently rejected, and the E2E `mockAgent.ts` payload types. Tests: 240 backend (+7), 126 agent (+2), mutation-tested both sides — removing the nonce from a message fails the #16 regression, the property test, **and** the golden vector (proving the cross-language lockstep does its job); signing a nonce without emitting it, and reusing one nonce for every emit, each fail their own test. **Verified live** after restarting the backend and republishing the agent: five back-to-back `run_task` calls at 27–60ms each (the second used to hang 15s), with #15's honest disabled-task message still intact as a control. Honest scope note: *the replay guard still catching real replays* is verified at the unit level (same nonce ⇒ same signature ⇒ rejected), not live — replaying a real socket frame would need MITM tooling. *(2026-07-15)*

- **MCP surface expansion — the management verbs, and the gating decision behind them** (P3 — completes *MCP surface expansion*; surface **7 → 14 tools**): an assistant could create and run tasks but not **manage** them — it couldn't answer *"did last night's job work?"*, park a task, fix a wrong command, or say what was actually registered. Seven new tools, each wrapping an **already-existing** route with **no new backend logic**: `create_native_task`, `get_task_history`, `export_task`, `set_task_status`, `update_task_schedule`, `update_task_action`, `delete_task`. The wrappers were mechanical; **the gating was the real work**, exactly as the item predicted. **The three decisions:** (1) **tiered gating** — read-only + reversible ungated, only the irreversible `delete_task` gated behind `TASKHUB_MCP_ALLOW_DESTRUCTIVE`, **absent** from `tools/list` when off (a visible tool is one the model plans around; a capability that announces itself then refuses burns turns on a door that doesn't exist), failing **closed** on any non-exact value. Gating *every* mutating verb was rejected for conflating irreversible with reversible: `set_task_status` is the honest way to park a task, and gating it pushes an agent toward cronning a task into silence (#14) — **a gate that makes the safe path harder than the unsafe one is worse than no gate**. The gate is an **env var, not a `confirm: true` param**, because a param is filled in by the model — the caller assuring *itself* it's sure, which is the missing deliberation rather than a substitute for it; an env var is out-of-band, mirroring the posture the elevated agent already takes toward its own caller. (2) **One tool per verb** — collapsing the three `update_*` saves two tools and costs the per-verb warnings that make them safe; **the descriptions are the safety mechanism**. (3) **Low-confidence schedules → fix the backend warning, don't refuse in the wrapper** (see the separate fallback-warning entry) — refusing would put policy in `mcp-server/` (§11a) and protect only MCP callers. `create_native_task` stayed separate from `create_task` on inspection: `POST /api/tasks/native` takes a full job spec where `POST /api/tasks` takes a command string the connector can only turn into an HTTP GET. One real implementation find: `export_task` needed a **raw-bytes client path**, because the route delivers UTF-16 LE + BOM (the only encoding Windows re-imports) and axios would have handed back mojibake. Tests: **49 new (124 total)**, mutation-tested — removing the gate, decoding the export as UTF-8, wiring undefined optionals, or dropping either honesty warning each fail a test; the mutation pass **caught a weak test of my own** (an alternation that survived deleting two-thirds of the guidance) which was then tightened. **Verified live over stdio** against the real backend + agent: the tool list changes with the gate, then create → disable → re-schedule (`0 18 * * 1-5`) → re-point the command → export readable XML → run → delete, with `\TaskHub` auto-pruning afterwards. Mirror surfaces updated in the same change per §11a (both README tool tables, both config tables, `.env.example`, `SKILL.md`, and the `task-authoring.md` §5 capability table — which had gone from "❌ roadmap" to wrong the moment these landed). *(2026-07-15)*

- **`run_task` on a disabled task reports the reason instead of a 15s "Agent trigger timeout"** (agent; found by driving the new MCP management verbs live): the agent's `task:run` replied **only on success** — `RunTask` returning false wrote to a console nobody reads, and a thrown COM error (a disabled task makes `task.Run()` throw `0x80041326` in ~7ms) was swallowed by the catch. With no reply the backend could only hit its 15s timeout and blame the **transport** for a **task-state** problem with a one-click fix. Every other verb already had an explicit ack/error; `task:run` was the outlier. Now every *accepted* command answers and the answer carries the reason (`23ms` → *"The task is disabled… Enable the task first"*); the **one** deliberate silence is a signature-verification failure, where a forger should learn nothing. 3 new xUnit tests (**124** agent total), mutation-tested. Required an agent republish; verified live after one. **Diagnosis note worth keeping:** the elevated agent discards stdout, so this was only visible by running the published exe in the **foreground** — it connects and replaces the elevated one in the backend's registry, so it handles the commands while you read its output. Logged as troubleshooting **#15**. *(2026-07-15)*

- **The hourly-fallback warning names the cost** (P3 honesty): an unrecognized cron is replaced with a hard-coded hourly trigger not derived from the input, but the warning said times *"might not align 100%"* — describing **drift**, so once-a-year → ~8,760-runs-a-year read as a rounding error at `confidence: 0.7`. Now states the schedule is **REPLACED — not approximated**, names the frequency, states that the fallback only ever runs **more** often than asked, and points at disable-don't-cron. Fixed **once in the backend** so UI, Apply, and MCP all benefit rather than special-casing a surface. 3 new backend tests (**233** total), mutation-tested against the old wording — including a guard that the genuinely-approximate `*/7` step **keeps** its drift language, since both paths share a `0.7` score and the wording is the only thing telling them apart. Residual recorded: the **score** still doesn't distinguish them, so `>= 0.7` accepts a replacement — read the trigger, not the score (#14). *(2026-07-15)*

- **TaskHub landing page** (Go-public *Launch surface* — first pass): a public, **rich** landing page for the project, built self-contained (`landing-site/index.html` — inline CSS + vanilla JS, no build/CDN) in the same dark "automation control catalog" aesthetic as the gallery: an editorial hero + product pitch ("one pane of glass for every scheduled task"), a why-TaskHub feature strip, three prominent **destination cards** linking the project's public pieces — the **app repo** (`github.com/michaelschecht/taskhub`, flagged "private until launch"), the **template registry repo** (`github.com/michaelschecht/taskhub-registry`), and the **template gallery** (`mikesailab.com/taskhub-registry/`) — a "how it fits together" flow band, dark/light toggle, and full responsiveness. Deployed to a **new public repo `michaelschecht/taskhub-site`** with GitHub Pages enabled; source of truth is `landing-site/` in this repo, mirrored by a new **`scripts/publish-landing.ps1`** (same pattern as the registry publish). **Live at `https://taskhub.mikesailab.com`** (and `https://mikesailab.com/taskhub-site/`) — verified 200, correct content, all three links resolve, 0 console errors, both themes clean. The vanity subdomain was initially blocked by a pre-existing **Vercel** DNS record on `taskhub` (`CNAME → cname.vercel-dns.com`, empty 404); the owner repointed it to `michaelschecht.github.io` on 2026-07-14, the CNAME was re-added and the Pages custom domain set, and the subdomain went live (HTTP immediately; HTTPS after Let's Encrypt cert issuance). Activation steps documented in `landing-site/README.md` › *Activating the subdomain*. *(2026-07-14)*

- **Template gallery site — part 1: the gallery frontend** (P3 — first slice of *Template gallery site + selective-import distribution*): a public, **rich multi-view** browse-and-import website over the `taskhub-registry` artifact, served at **`https://mikesailab.com/taskhub-registry/`**. Source of truth is a new **`registry-site/index.html`** in this repo — a single **self-contained** page (inline CSS + vanilla JS, **no build step, no external CDN/fonts** — offline-capable, CSP-safe, matching the content-addressed static philosophy) with a **hash-router** (works on GitHub Pages, deep-linkable) driving four views. Deliberate aesthetic: an *"automation control catalog"* — dark, textured (gradient-mesh + SVG grain), a monospace-forward technical identity (ids/cron/commands/stats in mono, fitting a catalog of scheduled commands), violet brand accent with a **phosphor-green "runnable" signal**, editorial type scale. The views: **(Home)** an editorial hero + live stat strip (templates / categories / runtimes / one-click-ready counts), **curated collection** cards (Script Starters, Developer Pack, AI & Agents, Backup & Cleanup, Monitoring — each derived from the data with a count), featured templates, and a "how importing works" band. **(Browse)** a real filter experience — a sticky **facet rail** with checkbox Category / Platform / Runtime facets showing **cross-faceted counts** (each recomputed over the list narrowed by the others), a tag cloud, search, sort (Featured / Name / Category), active-filter pills, and a card grid; the rail becomes a slide-in drawer under 860px. **(Collections)** an index of the curated bundles + a per-collection page. **(Template detail — `#/t/<id>`, shareable)** a full page: breadcrumb, hero, a **schedule card** with a **humanized cron** ("Weekdays at 6:00 PM UTC" from `0 18 * * 1-5`), a **placeholder-highlighted** command block with copy, a **parameter table** (type/required/help/default/`select` options), an honest **creatable-vs-manual targets** row, and an aside with **Download JSON** / **Copy JSON** (→ the app's shipped **Templates → Import** path, `POST /api/templates/import`, same schema + `{{placeholder}}` validation), a meta panel, and **related templates**. Plus an "Import guide" modal, dark/light theme toggle (persisted), responsive mobile nav, toasts, and loading/empty/error states. Published by extending **`scripts/publish-registry.ps1`** to mirror `registry-site/*` (excluding its dev `README.md` — the public repo owns its house-style front page) into the public clone **root** alongside the generated `index.json`, so the gallery ships in lockstep with the registry JSON, no separate deploy. **Verified live in a browser** against the real 40-template artifact: all four views render; Home stats correct (40 / 6 / 11 / 29); Browse category filter → 9 dev-workflow with platform counts recomputing (Cross-platform 3 / Windows 6 / macOS 0 under AI Agent), search "codex" → 3, active pills + clear-all work; a detail page shows a humanized weekday schedule, placeholder-highlighted command, param table, related templates, and a working Download (valid single-template JSON blob + toast); both themes clean; **0 uncaught exceptions** across full navigation (a caught clipboard-in-unfocused-tab rejection surfaces as an error toast). Parts 2 (curated **core** vs. gallery-only **extended** split — stop auto-syncing the whole registry into every local DB) and 3 (one-click **"Add to my TaskHub"** deep-link/protocol handoff) remain open. *(2026-07-14)*

- **Frontend refactor** (P3 — quality/UX pass, shipped as five browser-verified slices): broke up the frontend's biggest structural debts and closed the outstanding UX/a11y gaps. **(1) Accessible modal foundation + custom confirm dialog** — a new `components/ui/Modal.tsx` (focus trap, Escape, `role=dialog/alertdialog` + `aria-modal`/labelling, body scroll lock, focus restoration, portal) and a promise-based `hooks/useConfirm.ts`/`ConfirmProvider.tsx` (mirrors the ToastProvider split) replace the two remaining native `confirm()` gates — task **run** (`Dashboard`) and task **delete** (`TaskModal`), the latter with a red danger tone; the E2E run flow now clicks the custom Run confirm instead of accepting a native dialog. **(2) Modal shell adopted app-wide** — all 8 modals (Help, Import, CreateTask, CloneTask, ApplyTemplate, EditSchedule, EditAction, TaskModal) now render through the shared `Modal`, so Escape/focus-trap/ARIA/scroll-lock are universal; form modals keep `closeOnBackdrop=false` to preserve unsaved input. A module-level **modal stack** ensures only the top-most modal handles Escape/focus — so the delete-confirm over the task modal (or an edit modal over it) closes top-first instead of all at once (verified live). **(3) Mobile sidebar drawer** — below `md` the sidebar is an off-canvas drawer (hamburger toggle + dimmed backdrop + a mobile close button, closes on nav-select and Escape) and `main` padding tightens to `p-4 md:p-10`, finally meeting the **`<375px`** first-class-mobile requirement (verified at 360px). **(4) Router + deep links** — added `react-router-dom` and wrapped the app in `BrowserRouter`; the active section (`/`, `/templates`, `/platforms`, `/settings`) and open detail modals now live in the URL (bookmarkable, browser back/forward), with `/tasks/:id` opening the task detail and `/templates/:id` the apply modal — the `activeTab`/`selectedTask`/`applyTarget` local state is now URL-derived. Direct deep-links load via Vite's SPA fallback (verified for `/settings`, `/tasks/:id`, `/templates/:id`). **(5) `Dashboard.tsx` split** — the 1,830-line monolith is now a **248-line shell** (routing + task/sync/category mutations + top-level modal wiring) plus three focused modules under `src/screens/`: `DashboardScreen.tsx` (698 — faceted chips, search, grid/list/kanban/schedule views), `TemplatesScreen.tsx` (746 — the template library + apply flow), `PlatformsScreen.tsx` (167). Pure structural move, no behavior change; each screen carries only the imports it uses. Verified across all slices: 69 frontend unit tests (+ new ConfirmProvider a11y suite + Sidebar drawer tests + updated TaskModal/Import portal-query tests), build, lint, and live browser checks (custom confirm + cancel, nested-Escape, the 360px drawer, all deep-links, and all three extracted screens rendering with 0 console errors). Optional follow-up logged: route-level code-splitting (the single bundle now trips Vite's 500 kB hint). *(2026-07-13)*

- **MCP server** (P3 — highest-leverage Phase-6 item; thin wrapper over existing routes): a new `mcp-server/` package exposes the TaskHub REST API to any MCP host over **stdio**, so Claude Code / Claude Desktop / Codex / Cursor can drive TaskHub in natural language. **5 tools**, each a thin call through a small axios client (`src/client.ts`) into an existing route — no new business logic, so owner scoping, no-shell `{executable,args[]}` structuring, signed agent commands, and cron→trigger conversion all stay server-side where they're already tested: **`list_tasks`** (`GET /tasks`, with client-side platform/status/category/search filters), **`run_task`** (`POST /tasks/:id/run`), **`list_templates`** (`GET /templates` — surfaces each template's id, tags, target platforms, default schedule, and declared `{{placeholder}}` params so an agent can discover what to fill), **`create_task_from_template`** (`POST /templates/:id/apply` — server fills placeholders from raw `parameters`, converts the cron, registers via the signed agent; gated to the creatable platforms Windows + TaskHub-native), and **`convert_schedule`** (`POST /tasks/preview` — validate/convert a 5-field UTC cron to a native trigger with a 0–1 confidence score + lossy-conversion warnings). Auth is a single user JWT via `TASKHUB_TOKEN` (`Authorization: Bearer`), base URL via `TASKHUB_API_URL` — the server acts entirely as that one user (a per-user pairing flow replaces the hand-minted token once the Go-public account system lands). Honest failures: the client normalizes an API error into the backend's own message + status (`Task not found`, `HTTP 409 …`) and every tool returns `isError: true` rather than throwing; all logging goes to stderr so stdout stays a clean JSON-RPC stream. Built on the stable `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.29 (`McpServer` + `StdioServerTransport`, zod input schemas), TypeScript strict/ESM matching the backend. **Deliberately not wired into the repo's root `.mcp.json`** — that's Claude Code's dev tooling; this is a product component users point their own host at (README documents `claude mcp add` + Claude Desktop JSON). Verified live end-to-end against the running backend: MCP handshake + `tools/list` shows all 5; `list_tasks` returned 285 real tasks, `list_templates` surfaced git templates with correct per-param names, `convert_schedule` handled a valid cron (confidence 1 + trigger) and an invalid one (score 0 + warning), and the dummy-token path surfaced the real `HTTP 403 Invalid or expired token`. `tsc` build clean. Side-effecting tools (`run_task`, `create_task_from_template`) were not fired live to avoid running/registering real Windows tasks — identical plumbing to the verified read/preview paths. *(2026-07-13)*

- **TaskHub-native schedule editing** (P2 native follow-up): TaskHub-native tasks with a cron schedule now expose **Edit Schedule** in the task modal. `PATCH /api/tasks/:id/schedule` updates the backend-owned scheduler directly, recomputes `nextRunTime` via `cron-parser`, preserves `metadata.job`, and stores the cron in metadata for export/template parity; semantically invalid 5-field cron expressions are rejected before the task is changed. The same modal still handles Windows cron tasks through the signed agent path. Verified: targeted backend integration + frontend modal test. *(2026-07-13)*

- **Settings runtime backend URL override** (P2 Settings follow-up): Settings → About → API origin is now editable. `VITE_API_URL` remains the default, but the dashboard can save a browser-local override (`localStorage` `taskhub.apiOrigin`) without rebuilding. The runtime origin store updates Axios, the backend outage banner, and the Socket.IO `/ui` live-update connection; Reset returns to the env default and Reset all preferences clears the override. Agent pairing remains deferred to the real account/pairing flow. Verified: frontend 59 unit. *(2026-07-13)*

- **AI Pack templates — Codex** (P2 — the AI/CLI pack, Codex slice): **3** real, creatable Windows templates that run the **Codex CLI** unattended via `codex exec`: Codex Headless Run (`ai-codex-headless-run`), Codex Repo Digest (`ai-codex-repo-digest`), and Codex Auto-Fix Workspace (`ai-codex-autofix-workspace`). The safe path was validated against the current Codex manual + installed CLI (`codex exec --help`, v0.144.1) and smoke-tested live with `codex --ask-for-approval never exec --sandbox read-only --ephemeral ...` returning a sentinel response without the interactive UI. Templates use explicit no-prompt approval, explicit sandbox (`read-only` by default; `workspace-write` only for the higher-trust autofix template), `--ephemeral`, `--color never`, `-C` for repo root, `-o` for final-message capture, and PowerShell stream redirection for full logs. Tagged `ai`/`llm`/`cli`/`agents`/`codex`; registry rebuilt **37 -> 40** and covered by the whole-catalog resolvability/drift tests. *(2026-07-13)*

- **AI Pack templates — Claude Code** (P2 — the AI/CLI pack, Claude-Code-only first slice): **4** `ai-agent` use-case templates that schedule the **Claude Code CLI to run unattended** as real, *creatable* Windows tasks (`compatibleTargets: ['windows']`) — distinct from the honest-manual `ai-prompt` link starters. This clears the roadmap's **"safe non-interactive execution path"** gate for Claude Code, with the invocation verified against the current CLI docs (cli-reference / headless / permission-modes): **headless print mode** (`claude -p '{{prompt}}'`), permissions fenced by **`--permission-mode dontAsk`** (auto-denies anything not pre-approved, so a scheduled run never hangs on an approval prompt — the safe choice over `--dangerously-skip-permissions`), an explicit **user-scoped `--allowedTools`** allowlist, **`--bare`** (ignore local `.claude/` for machine-independent runs), `--max-turns` + `--model`, and **captured output**. `claude` is a native Windows binary but redirection needs a shell, so — like the npm pack — a shell is opted into explicitly; here **PowerShell** (`-NoProfile -Command "…"`) so the multi-word prompt and tool list ride as **single-quoted strings** inside the one `-Command` arg (the same double-quote-outer/single-quote-inner nesting the webhook starter uses; the "avoid single quotes in the prompt" caveat matches git-autocommit). `claude` is invoked bare on PATH (as git/npm/docker are). The four: **Claude Code Headless Run** (`ai-claude-headless-run` — the flexible primitive; read-only `Read,Grep,Glob` default scope), **Claude Code Repo Digest** (`ai-claude-repo-digest` — read-only, printed digest → dated `.md` report), **Claude Code Auto-Fix & Commit** (`ai-claude-autofix-commit` — higher-trust edit + scoped-git scope, labeled), and **Claude Code Log Cleanup** (`ai-claude-log-cleanup` — the roadmap's "agent log cleanup"; prunes old run logs). Every entry tagged `ai` + specifics (`llm`, `cli`, `agents`, `claude-code`, `report`, `git`, …) via the tags model; new ids use the plain-kebab `ai-*` form. Registry rebuilt **33 → 37** and the drift test kept green; the whole-catalog resolvability sweep now covers all four (each PowerShell command tokenizes to a single structured action). Codex slice shipped later the same day (see the Codex entry above). Verified: backend **192 unit** (+1 AI-tag guard; counts 33→37 across catalog + registry tests) + `tsc` clean, `npm run registry:build` regenerated `registry/` (37 files). **Published** to the public `taskhub-registry` repo via `scripts/publish-registry.ps1` — the hosted index (`https://mikesailab.com/taskhub-registry`) verified serving all 37 templates and the new `ai-claude-*` files; the live app picks the pack up on its next catalog sync (≤30 min), no redeploy. *(2026-07-13)*

- **Developer Pack templates** (P2 — the first content pack on the tags model): 9 development-focused use-case templates added to the bundled catalog and rebuilt into the registry artifact (24 → **33** templates). **Git hygiene ×3** — Git Fetch & Prune (`git -C "{{repoPath}}" fetch --all --prune`, cross-platform), Git Repo Maintenance (`git maintenance run`, weekly), and Git Auto-Commit & Push (PowerShell `add -A; commit; push` — spaced paths/messages ride as PS single-quoted strings inside the one `-Command` arg, per the existing webhook-starter quoting pattern). **Node toolchain ×3** — Dependency Update Check (`npm outdated > report`, with honest help text: npm exits non-zero exactly when updates exist, so a "failed" run *is* the signal), Nightly Build (`npm run {{script}}` with captured log), and Scheduled Test Run (`npm test` with captured log) — all via explicit `cmd.exe /c` opt-in (npm is a `.cmd`, and redirection needs a shell), matching the pg_dump escape-hatch convention. **Build/infra ×3** — .NET Build (`dotnet build`, direct no-shell exec, cross-platform), Docker Cleanup (`docker system prune -f`), and Docker Compose Self-Heal (`docker compose -f "{{composeFile}}" up -d` on an interval — the dogfooded pattern TaskHub's own stack uses). Every entry carries the `dev` tag plus specifics (`git`, `npm`, `build`, `test`, `ci`, `docker`, `hygiene`, …) so the pack lights up the new Tags facet; new ids use the plain-kebab registry form (`dev-*`) per the v1 spec (§7), with the legacy-id test updated to pin exactly the 24 pre-registry `tpl_*` rows. Also added a **whole-catalog resolvability sweep** to the unit suite — every bundled `commandTemplate` must resolve through the real Apply pipeline (`resolveTemplateParams` + `substituteStructuredCommand`) with synthesized params, so a typo'd `{{placeholder}}` can never ship in catalog content again. Structured actions spot-checked with realistic spaced-path values (compose file / .sln / repo path each stay exactly one argument; the npm shell lines stay one `/c` arg). Verified: backend 191 unit (+2: dev-tag guard, resolvability sweep; counts updated 24→33) + 74 integration, `tsc` clean, `npm run registry:build` regenerated `registry/` (33 files, drift test green). **Published** to the public `taskhub-registry` repo via `scripts/publish-registry.ps1` — the hosted index (`https://mikesailab.com/taskhub-registry`) verified serving all 33 templates and the new `dev-*` files; the live app picks the pack up on its next catalog sync (≤30 min interval), no redeploy. *(2026-07-13)*

- **Template tags model** (P2 — the shared prerequisite for the Developer Pack + AI/CLI template packs): templates now carry **free-form `tags`**, distinct from the single `category` enum, so a template can be tagged `git`/`build`/`ai` without overloading `TemplateCategory`. New `tags String[] @default([])` column on `Template` (migration `20260713193924_add_template_tags`); tags are carried through the whole catalog pipeline — the Registry v1 schema already had `tags`, and `normalize` (registry→DB) + `denormalize` (DB→registry, for export) now preserve them (round-trip verified). In the Templates tab, the previously **mislabeled "Tags" chip row was renamed to "Category"** (it was always the enum), and a **real, dedicated Tags facet** was added — multi-valued (a template contributes to every tag it carries), cross-faceted with OS + Category (each facet counts over the list narrowed by the other two), plus **per-card tag chips** and tags folded into template search. Verified: backend 189 unit + 73 integration (+1 tags round-trip: import-with-tags → `GET /templates` shows them → export includes them), frontend 55 + build + lint; **live end-to-end** — after regenerating the container's Prisma client + a boot `catalogSync`, all 24 templates surfaced tags (`ai`, `git`, `python`, `windows`, …) in the new facet and card chips (browser-verified). *(2026-07-13)*

- **Export existing tasks** (P2 — export a user's *actual* tracked tasks, distinct from template export): the task modal gained a per-task **Export** button. A **Windows** task exports as its native Task Scheduler **XML** through a new **read-only `task:export`** agent round-trip (`Win32TaskScheduler.ExportTaskXml` returns `Task.Xml` — byte-identical to `Export-ScheduledTask` / the UI's Export; unsigned, mirroring `task:list`, since it only reads). The route (`GET /api/tasks/:id/export`, owner-scoped) delivers it **UTF-16 LE + BOM** keeping the native `encoding="UTF-16"` declaration, so it re-imports through every Windows path (UI Import Task, `Register-ScheduledTask -Xml`, `schtasks /xml`) — a UTF-8 declaration was tried first and **rejected by the COM/string import** ("unable to switch the encoding"), verified against real Task Scheduler. A TaskHub-**native** task has no Windows XML form, so it exports as TaskHub **JSON** (`{ taskhubTaskVersion, exportedAt, task: { name, platform, category, schedule, job } }`) built straight from the DB row (no agent). Honest errors: agent-offline Windows export → 502; unsupported platform → 400; IDOR → 404. Granularity: **per-task** (the clear win); a whole-account/multi-select bulk export (needs a zip to bundle mixed XML+JSON) is deferred.
