The frontend downloads through the api client (auth header) using the server's `Content-Disposition` filename, and reads the error blob back to surface the real message. Verified: backend 189 unit (+2 connector) + 73 integration (+5: native JSON shape/headers, Windows-offline 502, unsupported 400, IDOR 404, 401), agent 71 (+2: `task:export` returns XML / not-found), frontend 55 + build + lint; the native XML retrieval + the UTF-16-vs-UTF-8 re-import behavior were validated live against real Task Scheduler. *(2026-07-13)*

- **User resources & onboarding** (P2 — recommended before going public): the in-app **Help Center** (`HelpModal`) was reworked to lead with a **Getting Started** walkthrough and a **Guides & Docs** section instead of just static tips. The walkthrough is a 4-step numbered path — **connect the Windows agent → import & categorize tasks → run & manage → use & grow templates** — self-contained in-app, with per-step deep-links to the fuller guides where useful. The Guides section links the real docs (UI User Guide, Windows Agent Setup Guide, Template Catalog spec — GitHub blob on `main`) plus the public **template registry** (browse/contribute). A dismissible **first-run banner** on the dashboard ("New to TaskHub? Take the 4-step tour") opens the Help Center and is remembered via a new `onboardingSeen` preference (`useSettings`, default false; dismiss or "Take the tour" both set it). Content is data (`frontend/src/data/onboarding.ts` — `GETTING_STARTED_STEPS` + `HELP_GUIDES`) so it's easy to edit. Existing Pro Tips + external Resources (crontab.guru, Windows API, …) kept below. Note: the guide deep-links resolve for the repo owner today (the app repo is private) — hosting the docs for a public audience stays in the Go-public checklist. Verified: frontend 55 unit (+3 `HelpModal` — walkthrough renders, every guide links new-tab with the right href, close works) + build + lint. *(2026-07-13)*

- **Save task as template** (P2 — grow the catalog from real tasks; the second non-reseed authoring path alongside import): the task detail modal gained a **Save as template** button (Windows + TaskHub-native tasks) that turns a real tracked task into a catalog template. New owner-scoped `POST /api/tasks/:id/save-as-template` derives a Registry v1 template from the task via `backend/src/catalog/templateFromTask.ts` — reconstructing the command from the task's metadata exactly like the modal's Action panel (native HTTP job → its URL; Windows single exec action → exe + args, exe quoted when it has whitespace so it re-tokenizes to the same `{executable,args}`; plain stored command verbatim), inferring the runtime from the executable basename, mapping the free-text category to a v1 category, and stamping a fresh `tpl_saved_<12hex>` id so a save never collides with or overwrites a catalog template — then routes it through the **same import pipeline** (`importTemplates`: schema + `{{placeholder}}` resolvability + upsert-by-id), so a saved template is validated exactly like imported content. A task with no cron-expressible schedule (boot/logon trigger) or no capturable command is an honest 400; the button is disabled with a reason when the task has no schedule. The frontend mutation invalidates `['templates']` so the new template shows on the Templates tab immediately. Verified: backend 187 unit (+8: derivation from exec-action/quoted-path/native-job/plain-command, overrides, no-schedule/no-command/multi-action rejections) + 68 integration (+5: Windows + native save → appears in `GET /templates`, no-schedule 400, IDOR 404, 401), frontend 52 + build + lint. *(2026-07-13)*

- **Self-heal tasks no longer flash a PowerShell window** + **self-heal dedup** (P2 — agent-hygiene bug, observed 2026-07-13): the `\Task-Hub\` self-heal scheduled tasks popped a visible PowerShell/conhost window on every recurring fire. Root cause confirmed: `powershell.exe -WindowStyle Hidden` still creates a conhost window *then* hides it (a flash), and **two** overlapping tasks (`TaskHubStack` every 5 min + `TaskHubAgent` every 10 min, both ultimately running the idempotent `taskhub.ps1 up`) doubled it. Fix: a new **`scripts/startup-task/run-hidden.vbs`** launches PowerShell via `WScript.Shell.Run(cmd, 0, False)` — created hidden from the start, and `wscript.exe` is itself windowless, so nothing appears — **verified live** (the shim ran a probe script hidden with no window). `Register-TaskHubStack.ps1` now (1) registers `TaskHubStack` (recurring self-heal) to launch via the shim and (2) **repairs** any existing `TaskHubAgent` to a flash-free, **logon-only** bootstrap (Docker + `up`) via the shim — removing its redundant recurring repetition so only one task re-runs `up` on an interval. `Set-TaskHubRepetition.ps1` is deprecated (no-op without `-Force`; adding a repetition to TaskHubAgent now duplicates TaskHubStack). Applying to a live install is one elevated re-run of `Register-TaskHubStack.ps1`. Both PS scripts parse clean; README updated. *(2026-07-13)*

- **Empty `\TaskHub\` scheduler folder auto-pruned** (P2 — honesty/cleanup, observed 2026-07-13): TaskHub-created Windows tasks register under `\TaskHub\<name>`, but `task:delete` removed the task and left the now-empty folder behind (Task Scheduler doesn't auto-prune empty folders — confirmed live: `\TaskHub` was sitting empty while `\Task-Hub` held the 2 infra tasks). The agent's `Win32TaskScheduler.DeleteTask` now prunes the `\TaskHub\` folder when deleting its last task (`TryPruneTaskHubFolder`): guarded to *our* folder only (name `"TaskHub"`, never root or the hyphenated `\Task-Hub\` infra folder), only when it holds no tasks and no subfolders, and best-effort (a folder-delete failure never turns a successful task delete into an error). The folder stays lazily created (only exists while it holds tasks). **Verified live** against real Task Scheduler: create folder+task → non-empty delete correctly blocked (the `Count` guard avoids that call) → delete task → folder empty → pruned → gone. Agent builds clean, 69 tests pass. *(Naming consolidation of the near-duplicate `\Task-Hub\` (infra) vs `\TaskHub\` (user tasks) folders is deferred — a rename in either direction needs either a large backend/test/frontend blast radius or coordinated elevated re-registration of the live infra tasks; the auto-prune makes the overlap transient, and the distinct roles are now documented in the agent code + scripts README.)* *(2026-07-13)*

- **Template import/export** (P2 — Templates-tab; the two non-reseed ways the catalog grows): a signed-in user (or an agent) can now **export** the catalog to a portable Registry v1 JSON file and **import** templates back through the same validation Apply uses — no reseed/redeploy. Built on the registry v1 schema: a new **`denormalize.ts`** lowers a Prisma `Template` row back to a v1 template (the inverse of `normalize.ts`, with the enum inverse maps derived from the forward maps so they can't drift), and export reads the **DB** (what the user actually sees, incl. previously-imported rows) via `exportCatalog.ts`. New routes: **`GET /api/templates/export`** (whole catalog / `?ids=a,b` subset as an `{ taskhubCatalogVersion, exportedAt, templates[] }` bundle, or `?id=x` as a single bare v1 object, both as `Content-Disposition` downloads) and **`POST /api/templates/import`** (accepts a bundle, a bare array, or a single object). Import is defense-in-depth for *untrusted, executable* content: each entry is (1) schema-validated against `registryTemplateSchema`, (2) put through the **same `{{placeholder}}` resolvability check Apply enforces** (`importCatalog.ts` synthesizes a value per declared param and drives the real `resolveTemplateParams` + `substituteStructuredCommand` — an undeclared placeholder or empty command is rejected), then (3) normalized + **upsert-by-id** into the shared catalog (favorites/applied tasks untouched, mirroring `catalogSync`). Bad entries don't fail the batch — each is reported per-item in `errors`, so a hand-authored file with one typo still imports the rest; the route returns 400 only when nothing imported. Frontend: **Export**/**Import** buttons in the Templates header — Export downloads through the api client (auth header rides along, not a bare `<a href>`), Import reads a picked file, POSTs, and surfaces an honest per-item toast (`N added, M updated, K skipped`, first issue shown), then invalidates `['templates']`. This is the agent-authoring path too: an agent can be pointed at the v1 schema to draft/bulk-edit templates and POST them to `/import`. Verified: backend 179 unit (+12: denormalize round-trip over all 24 bundled templates, import validation/partial-batch/shape parsing) + 63 integration (+8: export bundle/single/404, full export→wipe→import round-trip, updated-vs-created, partial-success 200 vs all-invalid 400, unfillable-placeholder 400, 401s), frontend 52 + build + lint. (Live API smoke deferred — the running self-heal stack is on the pre-change build; the integration suite drives the identical `createApp()` + real Postgres.) Pairs with the still-open **Save task as template**. *(2026-07-13)*

- **Favorite templates** (P2 — Templates-tab personalization): a signed-in user can now **star/unstar** any template from the grid card, list row, and kanban card, and filter the Templates tab with a **Favorites** toggle (live count badge); favorites float to the top of each group (`byFavoriteThenName`). Favorites are **per-user** — a new `TemplateFavorite` join model (`@@unique([userId, templateId])`, cascade-delete with the template) keyed by `userId_templateId`, so favoriting never mutates the shared seeded catalog. `GET /api/templates` enriches each row with a per-user `isFavorite` (one extra `templateFavorite.findMany` in parallel); new idempotent `POST`/`DELETE /api/templates/:id/favorite` (upsert / deleteMany, 404 on an unknown template). The star toggle is **optimistic** — `onMutate` flips `isFavorite` in the `['templates']` cache immediately, rolls back on error, reconciles on settle. This is the honest personalization signal that replaced the removed fake `upvotes`. Verified: live API flow (favorite → list shows true → unfavorite → false → 404 on bad id), a real-browser Playwright E2E (star a card → Favorites filter → unstar), and per-user isolation in integration tests (user A's favorite doesn't leak to user B). Backend 148 unit + 55 integration (+6 favorites), frontend 52 + 7 E2E (+1) + lint/build; migration `20260712020534_add_template_favorites`. *(2026-07-11)*

- **Edit Windows task actions/settings from TaskHub** (P2 — third/final slice of the Windows management pass): the Action detail panel gained an **Edit** button that opens an `EditActionModal` (command, working directory, description, run level) and changes a Windows task's action + settings through a new signed `task:update` → `task:updated` round-trip. New owner-scoped `PATCH /api/tasks/:id/actions` structures the command server-side into a no-shell `{ executable, args[] }` action (`toStructuredAction` — same injection-proof model as create; empty executable is a 400) and calls `WindowsAgentConnector.updateActions`; the agent verifies signature/freshness/replay, then rebuilds **only** the exec action and sets Description + RunLevel by reusing the live `TaskDefinition` (`Win32TaskScheduler.UpdateTaskActions`: `Actions.Clear()` + re-add the direct `ExecAction`, set `RegistrationInfo.Description` / `Principal.RunLevel`, re-register the same definition), so **triggers, principal identity (run-as/logon type), and other settings are preserved**, and acks. Ordering is honest: the DB metadata (command/actions/description/runLevel) updates only after the platform confirms; an agent-offline edit is a 502 with the row intact, a native/unsupported platform is a 400. The whole change (action, working dir, description, run level) is signed into the HMAC — a new `task:update` `SignableCommand` variant with canonical `task:update|path|action(0x1F-joined)|workingDir|description|runLevel|ts`, pinned by a new cross-language golden vector in both suites (backend Vitest + agent xUnit assert the identical hex). Editing is gated in the UI to Windows tasks with exactly one reported exec action (multi-action tasks stay read-only with an honest reason); `enabled` remains owned by the enable/disable feature, and run-as user / logon type are deferred as riskier v2 edits. **Not breaking** (additive — existing signatures unchanged; an old agent just times the new command out to a 502). **Verified live end-to-end** against real Windows Task Scheduler: created a `\TaskHub\` daily task (`cmd.exe /c echo original`, 14:00 UTC), edited it to `powershell.exe -NoProfile -File "C:\TaskHubDF\edited script.ps1"` with a working dir + new description → Task Scheduler XML confirmed the ExecAction/WorkingDirectory/Description changed **with the quoted space-bearing path preserved as a single argument (no shell split)** and the **daily trigger untouched**; a `runLevel: highest` edit from the unelevated agent correctly hit `E_ACCESSDENIED` → mapped to a friendly 502 with the DB unchanged; then deleted. Backend 148 unit (+4) + 49 integration (+6), agent 69 (+3), frontend 51 (+2) + 6 E2E (+1, real signed round-trip through the mock agent) + lint/build. *(2026-07-11)*

- **Templates honesty pass + Tier-B parameterization** (P2 — Templates-tab items): three cleanups. **(1) Removed the fake `upvotes` signal** — the static seeded vote counts (42/128/…) were never real community votes, so the card badge, the upvotes-based sort, the seed values, the frontend `Template.upvotes` type, and the route's `orderBy: upvotes` are all gone (sort is now starters-then-name; the dormant DB column is left for a future migration). **(2) "Cross-platform targets are cosmetic" honesty fix (approach a)** — cards now frame `targetPlatforms` under a **"Compatible with"** label (creatable platforms highlighted, others muted with a `*`), and the Apply modal gates the platform picker to `CREATABLE_PLATFORMS` (Windows + TaskHub-native, in `frontend/src/platform.ts`); an uncreatable-only template (e.g. a Claude-only AI pattern) shows a disabled button + an honest "no agent or API yet — copy the command to set it up manually" note with Create disabled, instead of letting Apply silently fail. **(3) Parameterized the 4 Tier-B patterns** — Daily Database Backup / Weekly System Cleanup / Morning News Digest / GitHub PR Triage now carry `{{placeholder}}` `commandTemplate`s + `parameters` (reusing the same server-side per-token substitution as the starters), and the DB Backup pattern dropped its dishonest CLAUDE_CODE target (it's a Windows shell command). Verified live in the browser: DB Backup applies with 3 real inputs (Windows-only), and GitHub PR Triage (Claude-only) is correctly non-appliable with the honest note. Backend 144 unit + 43 integration, frontend 49 (ApplyTemplateModal test updated for the gating) + build + lint green; dev DB re-seeded. *(2026-07-10)*

- **Templates tab: view selector + Resources menu** (P2 — Templates-tab items): the Templates tab gained a **Grid / List / Kanban** view switcher (mirrors the dashboard's toggle) persisted to a new `settings.templateView` preference (`useSettings`) — Grid keeps the Starters/patterns card layout, List is a compact row layout (`TemplateListRow`), and Kanban lays out one horizontally-scrolling lane per Category/Tag (`TemplateKanban`, count per lane). Alongside it, a **Resources** dropdown (`TemplateResourcesMenu`) links curated sites/repos/docs, backed by a maintainable data module (`frontend/src/data/templateResources.ts`, sourced from `docs/resources/` + the catalog spec) with four sections (TaskHub docs, scheduler references, AI/agent workflows, template repositories) opening in new tabs. Verified live in the browser: all three views render (Kanban lanes AI Agent/Backup/Cleanup/Dev Workflow/Monitoring/Other with correct counts) and the Resources menu shows every section/link. Frontend build + lint + 49 unit tests green. *(2026-07-10)*

- **Edit Windows task schedules from TaskHub** (P2 — second slice of the Windows management pass): the previously no-op **Edit Schedule** button now opens an `EditScheduleModal` (reuses the New Task modal's cron presets, the `describeCron` local/UTC preview, and the live `/tasks/preview` conversion warnings) and changes a Windows task's schedule through a new signed `task:update_schedule` → `task:schedule_updated` round-trip. New owner-scoped `PATCH /api/tasks/:id/schedule` converts the cron to a structured trigger (`convertCronToWindowsTrigger`) and calls `WindowsAgentConnector.updateSchedule`; the agent verifies signature/freshness/replay, then rebuilds **only** the trigger via `Win32TaskScheduler.UpdateTaskSchedule` (pulls the live `TaskDefinition`, `Triggers.Clear()` + re-register the same definition, so **actions/principal/settings are preserved**) and acks. Ordering is honest: the DB row's `schedule`/`trigger` update only after the platform confirms; an agent-offline edit is a 502 with the row intact, a native task or unconvertible cron is a 400. Editability is gated to Windows tasks with a cron-expressible schedule (`task.schedule != null`) — boot/logon/event/on-demand triggers stay a read-only, disabled button with an honest tooltip. The trigger is signed into the HMAC (mirrors the `task:create` trigger coverage), pinned by a new cross-language golden vector in both suites. **Breaking** only in that the new command needs agent + backend together (additive — existing signatures unchanged; an old agent just times the new command out to a 502). Fixed a latent pre-existing type error surfaced by the build: `PlatformConnector.setTaskStatus` declared `{ success }` but the route/connector use `{ success, message }`. **Verified live end-to-end**: created a real `\TaskHub\` Windows task (Daily 08:00 UTC), edited it to Weekly Sunday 22:00 UTC via the API → Task Scheduler XML confirmed the trigger became `ScheduleByWeek`/Sunday at `15:00-07:00` (= 22:00 UTC Sunday) **with the `cmd.exe /c echo hi` action preserved**, then deleted it. Backend 144 unit (+4) + 43 integration (+5), agent 66 (+3), frontend 49 + 5 E2E (+1, real signed round-trip through the mock agent) + lint/build. *(2026-07-10)*

- **Windows task enable/disable from TaskHub** (P2): Exposed the `task:set_status` command as an interactive feature in the dashboard. The agent now returns an explicit `task:status_set` acknowledgement (with friendly Windows elevation/ACL error mapping); the backend waits for this acknowledgement and updates the database status and next run times accordingly, returning a 502/400 upon failure. Added a status toggle button inside the frontend's `TaskModal` dashboard overview card (complete with loading spinner support). Verified with 63 agent unit tests (+3), 140 backend unit tests (+1), and 38 integration tests (+5). *(2026-07-10)*

- **Apply-modal upgrades + Windows task-name guard** (P2): the Apply modal gained an **editable task-name field** (prefilled with the template name), **cron preset chips** (shared with the New Task modal via `frontend/src/utils/cronPresets.ts`), and a **human-readable schedule preview** (`describeCron` + the Settings Local/UTC mode — "Runs daily at 8:00 AM UTC" under the cron input). The real fix underneath: a server-side **Windows task-name guard** (`backend/src/utils/windowsTaskName.ts`) used by both `POST /tasks` and `POST /templates/:id/apply` — invalid names (Windows filename chars, control chars, >200) are a 400, and a name colliding with a tracked `\TaskHub\` task is a **409** instead of `RegisterTaskDefinition` silently overwriting the existing task. The apply route now also **upserts the created task immediately** (mirroring `POST /tasks`), so applied tasks appear on the dashboard without waiting for a sync and a back-to-back duplicate apply 409s. The collision check matches case-insensitively on the name column then compares the path exactly in JS — because Prisma's `mode: 'insensitive'` compiles to ILIKE where `\` is an escape character and a `\TaskHub\…` path silently never matches (found by the integration tests). Also fixed the stale New Task modal hint claiming commands run "via cmd.exe /c" (they run as a direct no-shell ExecAction since the P0 work), and the mock-agent E2E helper learned `task:delete` so the apply spec cleans up through the real signed delete path (repeat-run safe, verified 2× green). Multi-agent review caught and fixed before ship: the immediate-upsert would have wiped `metadata.job` on a TaskHub-native apply (now Windows-only, with a real-Postgres regression test), the `POST /tasks` fallback externalId (`\<name>`) didn't match where the agent registers (`\TaskHub\<name>`) which would have blinded the duplicate guard, trailing-dot names (Windows strips them on disk → silent collision) are now rejected, and the time-bearing cron preset labels say UTC. Verified live: apply with a custom name registered `\TaskHub\Apply Name Probe`, immediate duplicate apply → 409, invalid name → 400, API delete removed it from schtasks + DB. Backend 139 unit (+6) + 33 integration (+6), frontend 48 (+2) + 6 E2E + lint/build. *(2026-07-10)*
- **Agent `task:delete`** (P2 — the last missing CRUD op): `DELETE /api/tasks/:id` now deletes real Windows Task Scheduler entries through the agent. The backend emits a signed `task:delete` (new `SignableCommand` variant, per-session HMAC, new golden-vector case pinned by both suites) via `WindowsAgentConnector.deleteTask`; the agent verifies signature/freshness/replay, deletes with `Task.Folder.DeleteTask` (no shell), and acks `task:deleted`. Ordering is honest: the DB row is removed only after the platform confirms, an agent-offline delete is a 502 with the row intact, and deleting a task already removed natively is an idempotent success so stale rows clean up. Platforms without connector delete support keep the 400. `PlatformConnector` gained optional `deleteTask`; the task modal shows Delete for Windows tasks with scheduler-specific confirm copy. Not breaking (existing signatures unchanged — an old agent just times the new command out). Verified live: probe task created → API delete → gone from `schtasks` + DB; second probe deleted natively first → API delete cleaned the stale row. Dogfood follow-up: a real task registered by an elevated process (read-only user ACL) is correctly refused with `E_ACCESSDENIED`, now mapped to an actionable "requires administrator rights" message instead of the raw HRESULT (row survives; delete from an elevated Task Scheduler or run the agent elevated). Backend 133 unit (+4) + 27 integration (+3), agent 60 (+4), frontend 46 (+1) + lint/build. *(2026-07-10)*
- **Failure notifications** (P2 first slice): added backend server-side failure notification delivery for failed manual runs and failed scheduled TaskHub-native runs. New `FailureNotificationService` supports `generic`, `discord`, and `ntfy` webhooks via backend env (`TASKHUB_FAILURE_WEBHOOK_URL`, `TASKHUB_FAILURE_WEBHOOK_TYPE`, optional `TASKHUB_FAILURE_WEBHOOK_HEADERS_JSON`), queues delivery fire-and-forget, and swallows/logs delivery failures so a webhook outage never breaks task execution. Added unit coverage for no-op/config/payload/failure behavior and integration coverage proving manual + scheduled failures POST a webhook event. Updated [`artifacts/taskhub_failure_notifications_2026-07-10.md`](../artifacts/taskhub_failure_notifications_2026-07-10.md). Verified backend build, 129 unit tests, 24 integration tests, production audit 0. *(2026-07-10)*
- **Security audit + performance baseline** (P1 QA — final slice): audited auth/session, REST, agent socket, structured command execution, sync pruning, frontend storage/XSS-sensitive patterns, native HTTP jobs, and dependencies. Remediated production dependency advisories in `backend/package-lock.json` and `frontend/package-lock.json` (`ws`/Socket.IO transitive chain and `form-data`); post-fix production `npm audit` is 0 for both apps, and NuGet vulnerability checks are clean for the agent projects. Wrote [`artifacts/taskhub_security_audit_2026-07-10.md`](../artifacts/taskhub_security_audit_2026-07-10.md) and [`artifacts/taskhub_performance_2026-07-10.md`](../artifacts/taskhub_performance_2026-07-10.md). Local baseline: `GET /api/tasks` over 285 rows p50 74.1 ms / p95 96.9 ms; full 284-task Windows sync 1674.7 ms; frontend production bundle 436.62 kB JS raw / 128.91 kB gzip. Verification after remediation: backend 123 unit + 22 integration, frontend 45 unit + 6 E2E + build/lint, agent 56. *(2026-07-10)*
- **Resilience QA + stale-pruning guard** (P1 QA — fourth slice): hardened sync deletion safety after mock-agent soak exposed the failure mode directly. `TaskService.removeStaleTasks` now returns 0 without deleting when a connector returns an empty snapshot, and skips pruning for established platforms when the returned snapshot is suspiciously small (20+ tracked rows and fewer than 50% of IDs returned). Normal low-count pruning still works. Added unit tests for normal prune / empty snapshot / partial snapshot and real-Postgres integration tests for normal prune and "30 rows preserved when only 1 ID returns." Ran the full browser E2E suite and a short repeated mock-agent soak (`--repeat-each=3 --workers=1`, 12/12). Wrote [`artifacts/taskhub_resilience_2026-07-10.md`](../artifacts/taskhub_resilience_2026-07-10.md). *(2026-07-10)*
- **Mock-agent Playwright E2E suite** (P1 QA — third slice): added a deterministic browser-test agent under `frontend/tests/e2e/helpers/` that connects to the real backend with the production HMAC handshake, answers `task:list`, and records/responds to signed `task:run` and `task:create` commands. The new serial E2E spec covers: Import discovery with a mock Windows category, sync of an E2E task without stale-pruning existing local rows (the helper echoes current Windows external IDs), Run Now through the dashboard + native confirm, template apply through the real create socket command and structured action payload, offline status after agent disconnect/reload, and a `<375px` dashboard smoke. `frontend/eslint.config.js` now gives E2E helpers Node globals. Verified: Playwright 6/6, frontend lint, frontend Vitest 45. *(2026-07-10)*
- **Playwright E2E scaffold + dashboard smoke test** (P1 QA — second slice): stood up browser-based end-to-end testing in the frontend. Added `@playwright/test` + the Chromium binary, `frontend/playwright.config.ts` (testDir `tests/e2e`, `baseURL` overridable via `PLAYWRIGHT_BASE_URL`, trace + screenshot on failure, retry once in CI), and a `test:e2e` script; excluded `tests/e2e/**` from the Vitest config so the two runners don't collide (Vitest's default include otherwise grabs `.spec.ts`). The smoke suite (`tests/e2e/smoke.spec.ts`) drives real Chromium against the running dev stack: it loads the dashboard and asserts the "Manage N tasks…" subtitle — which only renders when the backend returns tasks, so one assertion covers frontend → backend → Postgres — then client-navigates to the template library. Playwright artifacts (`test-results/`, `playwright-report/`) are gitignored. Verified green (2 passed) against the live stack; Vitest still 45. **Deliberately a plumbing slice** — the full scenario set (Run Now, template apply, offline guard) needs a mock agent to answer the socket deterministically, which is the next step. *(2026-07-10)*

##### Security hardening (July 2026)

- **Integration test suite against real Postgres** (P1 QA — first slice; carries the IDOR regression tests deferred from P0): a new backend integration harness runs the *real* Express routes (via supertest) and services against a *real* Postgres, separate from the mock-Prisma unit suite. `src/app.ts` was extracted (`createApp()` — middleware + routes + error boundary) so tests drive the app without opening the agent socket or starting the native scheduler; `index.ts` still owns the HTTP/Socket.IO server and bootstrap. A dedicated `vitest.integration.config.ts` (serial, single fork) points at a `taskhub_test` database that `globalSetup` creates + `prisma migrate deploy`s, with a `beforeEach` TRUNCATE for per-test isolation; `*.integration.test.ts` is excluded from the unit config so `npm test` stays DB-free, and `npm run test:integration` runs the new suite (wired into CI with a Postgres service). **21 tests**: the **IDOR/multi-tenancy regressions** (the P0 deferral — user B gets 404 on GET/PATCH/DELETE/run/executions of user A's task, and mutations genuinely don't land; `GET /tasks` is owner-scoped; unauthenticated → 401), the **auth flow** (register → token authenticates a protected route; wrong password 401; duplicate email 400; Zod weak-password/bad-email 400), **config encryption at rest** (a config written through `serializeConfig` lands as `iv:tag:ciphertext` in the raw DB column with the plaintext secret absent, round-trips back, rejects a tampered payload, and still reads legacy plaintext object rows), and **data flow** (native-create computes a real `nextRunTime`; `TaskService.upsertTasks` persists a 250-task sync across batched `$transaction`s in order and idempotently; `removeStaleTasks` prunes). Audit finding surfaced while writing these: the archived Test_Plan's `429` login rate-limit isn't implemented (tracked in Go-public). Later P1 QA slices completed Playwright E2E, soak/resilience, security audit, dependency remediation, and performance baseline. Backend unit 121 + integration 21 at this slice. *(2026-07-10)*
- **Agent-auth hardening follow-ups** (closes the three residuals from the 2026-07-09 WebSocket-auth review): **(1) the `task:create` trigger is now signed.** The HMAC message gained a canonical trigger field (`canonicalizeTrigger` in `utils/scheduler-conversion.ts` / `AgentAuthenticator.CanonicalizeTrigger` in C# — a fixed-order pipe-joined form, null → `"none"`), so an on-path attacker on a plaintext connection can't rewrite a task's *schedule* in flight any more than its command. The agent parses the trigger *before* verifying and includes its canonical form in the checked message; the shared cross-language golden vector gained a null-trigger and a Weekly-trigger create case. **(2) Per-command replay guard.** The agent now rejects a `(ts, sig)` it has already accepted within the freshness window (bounded, self-pruning cache mirroring the server's handshake nonce cache), so a captured command frame can't be replayed on a plaintext link. **(3) Session-key reconnect overlap.** The agent kept a single mutable session key, so a command signed under the old connection that was still in flight when the socket rekeyed was false-rejected; it now keeps the current key (non-expiring) plus any superseded key for a `MaxSkew` grace window and verifies against either. A **latent bug caught in live verification**: the first cut expired *every* key after 120s, which silently broke all commands on any connection older than two minutes (the unit tests all verified within the window, so they passed) — fixed so the current key never expires. `emitSignedCommand`'s `extra` param was dropped (trigger is now a first-class signed field). **Breaking** — the create signature changed, so agent + backend must deploy together; the agent was republished for verification. Verified live end-to-end: rebuilt both sides, created a Windows task with a real Daily trigger (registered with the correct trigger + args), and ran a signed `task:run` **>120s after the handshake** to confirm the long-lived-key fix. Backend 121 tests (+3 trigger-canonical), agent 56 tests (+10: trigger canonical, replay, rekey-overlap, tampered-trigger-rejected). *(2026-07-10)*
- **Server-side per-token template substitution** (P1 command-handling follow-up — closes the last structured-command residual): the Apply modal used to substitute `{{placeholders}}` client-side and send the finished command string, which the backend then tokenized — so a parameter value with an unbalanced quote could split into extra literal args (harmless without a shell, but not what the author wrote). Now the client sends **raw parameter values** and the backend owns substitution (`backend/src/utils/templateCommand.ts`): the template is tokenized *first* (a new quote-tracking `parseCommandTokens` in `commandParser.ts`), then values substitute per-token — a quoted/composite placeholder (`-File "{{scriptPath}}"`, `--flag={{v}}`) is always exactly **one argument** regardless of its contents, while a bare placeholder token (`{{args}}`) keeps its intended multi-arg semantics, contained to that position. Raw params are validated server-side against the template's declared parameter definitions (required non-blank, `select` ∈ options, undeclared keys / non-string values rejected — each a specific 400). The resolved `{executable, args[]}` rides `CreateTaskOptions.action` to the Windows connector (no re-tokenization; still covered by the task:create HMAC), non-argv platforms (native HTTP jobs) get whole-string substitution, and the legacy pre-substituted `command` body field still works (deprecated). No agent change needed. **Verified end-to-end live**: applied the PowerShell Script starter from the browser with a spaced path → registered as one quoted `-File` arg; probed with `scriptPath = C:\nonexistent\x.ps1" & calc.exe` → registered as a **single backslash-escaped argument** to powershell.exe (no second process possible); API probes confirmed all four 400 paths. Noted while verifying (existing behavior, already backlogged in the P2 Apply-modal task-name item): re-applying a template with the same name silently overwrites the existing Windows task. Backend 116 tests (+23: `templateCommand` suite), frontend 45. *(2026-07-10)*
- **Structured command handling — no more `cmd.exe /c` shell wrap** (fourth P0 blocker done → **P0 security complete**): the agent registered every created task as `new ExecAction("cmd.exe", "/c " + command, null)` — the whole command string ran through a shell, so a template parameter substituted into it (e.g. a `{{scriptPath}}` of `x.ps1 & calc.exe`) was interpreted by cmd = command injection / RCE on the user's machine. Now the backend structures the resolved command into `{ executable, args[] }` server-side (`utils/commandParser.ts`, a quote-aware tokenizer) and the agent registers a **direct ExecAction** — no shell — quoting each argument by Windows `CommandLineToArgvW` rules (`ArgumentQuoting.cs`). Because no shell parses the line, a malicious parameter can at worst become a malformed argument to the *intended* executable; it can never launch a second process. The structured action is **covered by the task:create HMAC signature**: the signed message gained an action-canonical field (executable + args joined by 0x1F), computed identically on both sides and pinned by the shared cross-language golden vector (backend Vitest + agent xUnit both assert the new `createSig`). The `ITaskScheduler.CreateTask` contract now takes an `AgentExecAction` instead of a command string; commands that genuinely need shell features name the shell explicitly (two Tier-B seed patterns updated: `pg_dump … > backup.sql` and `del /q %temp%\\*` now use `cmd.exe /c "…"`). **Breaking** — agent + backend must be deployed together (the create signature changed). Verified: backend 93 tests (+10: `commandParser` + updated vectors), agent 46 tests (+7: `ArgumentQuoting` + updated create/vector tests). Residual (P1): the backend tokenizes the *client-substituted* command, so a parameter with an unbalanced quote could split into extra literal args (harmless without a shell) — robust per-parameter server-side substitution from raw params would remove even that. *(2026-07-09)*
- **Multi-tenancy route scoping + JWT fail-fast + dev-JWT rotation** (third P0 blocker done): the task API previously ran as a single hardcoded `cli_user_placeholder` and, worse, its by-id routes looked tasks up by `id` alone — so any authenticated caller could **list every user's tasks** (`GET /tasks` had no `where`), and read/patch/delete/**run** *anyone's* task by guessing an id (IDOR → the run path is remote command execution on another user's agent). Every task route now derives `userId` from the verified JWT (`req.user.id`, via the already-mounted `authenticateToken`), and all lookups are owner-scoped: `GET /tasks` filters `where:{userId}`; `PATCH`/`DELETE`/`POST /:id/run` use `findFirst({id, userId})` (404 on a miss); `GET /:id/executions` confirms task ownership before returning logs; create/native/health/sync/discover and the template **apply** route all attribute to the token user. Backend now **fails fast on a missing/weak `JWT_SECRET`** (the `|| 'fallback-secret'` default — which would have let anyone forge a token — is gone; `>= 16` chars required at boot, alongside the existing agent-secret and encryption-key checks). The **committed dev JWT was removed** from `frontend/src/api.ts` (a real leaked credential) and replaced with an env-sourced `VITE_DEV_TOKEN` (gitignored `.env.local`, no committed fallback), and **`JWT_SECRET` was rotated**, invalidating that leaked token. Added `.env.example` for both backend and frontend documenting every var. Templates stay a shared seeded catalog (global read is intentional — it's the curated library, all seeded under the placeholder user); route-level IDOR regression tests are folded into the pending real-Postgres integration suite (P1). Verified: backend typecheck + 83 tests, frontend typecheck + 40 tests, no committed token remains in tracked files. Remaining P0: per-OS structured command handling. The **git-history secret scrub** (the old token still lives in past commits, though rotation already killed it) is tracked under Go-public. *(2026-07-09)*
- **Encrypt `PlatformConnection.config` at rest** (second P0 blocker done): the connection `config` column (which holds API keys, agent IDs, and pairing secrets) was stored as plaintext JSON — a DB dump or backup leaked every credential. It's now encrypted at the application layer with the existing (tested) AES-256-GCM helpers, funneled through a single new module (`backend/src/auth/connectionConfig.ts`): `serializeConfig()` on the way in (write), `deserializeConfig()` on the way out (read). The Prisma column stays `Json`; the encrypted payload is stored as a JSON *string* in the canonical `iv:tag:ciphertext` form, and because encrypted configs are always strings while legacy configs were always objects, the stored JS type disambiguates the two — **legacy plaintext rows keep working with no migration** while every new write is encrypted. All 6 read sites (`createTask`/`applyTemplate`/`getHealth`/`syncTasks`×2/`runTask` across `routes/tasks.ts` + `routes/templates.ts`) and both write sites (the TASKHUB_NATIVE auto-provision in `index.ts` and the Windows auto-init in `/health`) now go through the helpers — no route touches `connection.config` directly, and the stale "*In a real system, we'd decrypt the config here*" comment is gone. A tampered/wrong-key payload throws (GCM auth failure is surfaced, not swallowed). Importing the module also gives the backend **fail-fast on a missing/short `ENCRYPTION_KEY`** at boot (the key must be exactly 32 chars). Verified: backend 83 tests (was 78; +5 new `connectionConfig` cases — round-trip, empty config, legacy-plaintext passthrough, null/undefined, tamper rejection). Remaining P0: multi-tenancy route scoping + dev-JWT rotation, per-OS structured command handling. *(2026-07-09)*
- **Agent WebSocket authentication** (first P0 blocker done): the agent↔backend Socket.IO channel — previously unauthenticated, so any process reaching port 3000 could drive `task:create`/`task:run` = RCE on the user's machine — is now authenticated on both ends. Backend `io.use()` middleware (`backend/src/ws/agentAuth.ts`) verifies an HMAC-SHA256 handshake over `(agentId, nonce, ts)` against a shared `AGENT_PAIRING_SECRET` (120s freshness, constant-time compare) and **rejects unauthenticated sockets before the connection handler runs**. State-changing commands are signed with a **per-session key** derived from the handshake nonce; the .NET agent (`AgentAuthenticator.cs`) verifies every `task:run`/`task:create`/`task:set_status` and drops anything with a bad signature or stale timestamp. Socket.IO CORS restricted to `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` (empty by default — only the non-browser agent connects today). Agent config (`TASKHUB_SERVER_URL` WSS-capable, `TASKHUB_PAIRING_SECRET`, `TASKHUB_AGENT_ID`) resolves from env **or** a gitignored `agent/TaskHub.Agent/appsettings.json` (env overrides the file; `appsettings.example.json` is the committed template, copied beside the published exe so no secret needs re-exporting each run); backend fails fast without a strong pairing secret. A shared cross-language HMAC golden vector is asserted by both the backend (Vitest) and agent (xUnit) suites so the two can't drift. A multi-agent code review also caught and fixed: an unauthenticated-frame crash in the agent's `task:run` handler (parse now inside the try), a reconnect race where a stale socket's late `disconnect` unregistered the live one (now guarded by socket identity), and added a server-side handshake **nonce replay cache** (a captured handshake can't be replayed within the freshness window to displace the agent). Verified: backend 78 tests, agent 39 tests; the Windows **auto-start / self-heal scheduled task** (`\Task-Hub\TaskHubAgent` → `Start-TaskHub.ps1`) was re-published and validated end-to-end — the published agent authenticates from its bundled config and the Windows connector reports `HEALTHY`, with the 10-min idempotent launcher confirmed not to spawn duplicates. **Breaking** — live agents must be rebuilt with the secret configured. Remaining P0: encrypt `PlatformConnection.config` at rest, multi-tenancy route scoping + dev-JWT rotation, per-OS structured command handling. Documented residuals (on-path attacker on plaintext transport only — mitigated by WSS, which the agent now supports): per-command replay within the 120s window, `task:create`'s `trigger` not covered by the signature, and a narrow shared-`SessionKey` reconnect false-reject. *(2026-07-09)*

##### Reliability & product sprint (July 2026)

- **Backend hygiene sweep** (P1): three long-standing structural gaps closed in one pass. **(1) Shared Prisma singleton** — 9 files each constructed their own `PrismaClient` (9 connection pools against one Postgres); everything now imports the single client from `src/db.ts` (the constructor-level mock in `TaskService.test.ts` still works unchanged). Also removed an entirely unused client in `auth/auth.ts`. **(2) One error boundary + Zod at the edges** — new `middleware/errorHandler.ts` (an `HttpError` class + a single Express error handler mapping `HttpError`→status, `TemplateParamError`→400, malformed-JSON→400, anything unexpected→logged generic 500 so internals never leak) and `middleware/validate.ts` (`validateBody(schema)` → field-qualified 400 from the first Zod issue, parsed/trimmed body replaces `req.body`). All three routers (tasks, templates, auth) dropped their per-route try/catch-500 boilerplate — handlers now throw and Express 5 forwards rejected promises to the boundary; intentional catches stay (per-connection sync/discover isolation, P2002 duplicate email). Zod schemas on every mutating body: task create / native create / patch / sync / preview, template preview / apply, auth register (email format + 8-char minimum now enforced) / login. `parameters` on apply stays schema-permissive by design — `resolveTemplateParams` owns the granular per-parameter errors. Bonus honesty fix: `POST /sync` with a non-array `categories` used to be silently ignored; it's now a clear 400. **(3) Batched sync upserts** — `TaskService.upsertTasks` ran one awaited upsert per task (a full Windows sync = 284 sequential round trips); it now chunks the upserts into `$transaction` batches of 100, preserving order and the exact same update/create semantics. Verified live: stack restarted, dashboard renders (285 tasks, both platforms Online), full 284-task sync in ~2.2s end-to-end, and 8 API probes confirmed the new 400/404 paths (field-qualified Zod messages, malformed JSON, HttpError 404s, TemplateParamError through the central handler with identical messages to the 2026-07-10 substitution verification). Backend 117 tests (+1 batching case), typecheck clean; no frontend changes needed. *(2026-07-10)*
- **Browser live updates (push instead of polling)** (P1): the dashboard now reflects changes the moment they happen — an agent sync, a native scheduled run, or an edit in another tab — instead of waiting for the 45s poll or a window-focus refetch. The backend exposes a JWT-authenticated, **receive-only** Socket.IO `/ui` namespace (`ws/uiChannel.ts`), kept fully separate from the pairing-secret-guarded agent channel (default namespace) so agent auth is untouched; browser clients authenticate with the user's token and join a per-user room. `notifyTasksChanged(userId)` is called on every task mutation (create / native-create / apply-template / sync / patch / delete / run) and once per affected user after each `NativeScheduler` tick; it emits `task:updated` to that user's room only. The frontend `useLiveTaskUpdates` hook (mounted in the dashboard) connects to `/ui` with the dev token, forces the WebSocket transport (skips the HTTP long-poll), backs off gently (30s max) when the backend is down, and invalidates `['tasks']` + `['connections']` on each push. Needs the frontend origin in `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` (documented; set for local dev + docker-compose). This is *lighter* than polling: one idle socket that only carries a message on a real change. Verified end-to-end (unauthenticated connect rejected; authenticated client received `task:updated` after a `POST /tasks/sync`); backend 93 tests, frontend 46 tests, both builds green. *(2026-07-09)*
- **Local-time schedule display in the task detail view** (P1): the parsed Schedule panel showed recurring times in UTC ("Daily at 9:00 AM UTC") because cron is stored in UTC — honest but not how a user reads their own schedule. `describeCron` (`utils/schedule.ts`) now takes the Settings Local/UTC mode; in **local** mode (the default) it converts the recurring clock time to the viewer's zone and rolls the day-of-week when the conversion crosses midnight (e.g. `0 2 * * 1` — Mon 02:00 UTC — reads "Weekly on Sunday at 9:00 PM" in New York). Conversion is anchored on a `now` reference instant so it reflects the current DST offset; interval shapes (every-N, hourly) are timezone-independent and unchanged. Wired through `TaskModal` via `useSettings`. The helper is ready to also power the P2 Apply-modal schedule preview. Also fixed a latent `tsc -b` narrowing error in `TaskModal.actionInfo` surfaced while building. Frontend 46 tests (+6 local-conversion cases incl. a pinned-timezone day-roll). *(2026-07-09)*
- **Live-agent schedule-normalization smoke test + midnight-cross day-of-week fix** (P1): dogfooded the synced-schedule normalization against real Windows Task Scheduler (282 tasks on the dev box). Confirmed Daily/Weekly triggers normalize to correct UTC crons with real next-run times and that non-cron triggers stay honestly scheduleless. The test surfaced a real bug: the agent's `TriggerReader` converted a weekly trigger's *time* to UTC but left its *day-of-week* on the local day, so a trigger crossing midnight in UTC reported the wrong day (`Weekly-Analysis`, Sun 23:45 PDT = Mon 06:45 UTC, showed `45 6 * * 0` instead of `45 6 * * 1` — and disagreed with its own next-run time, which was Monday). Fixed by rolling the day-of-week by the same local→UTC day shift (mirroring the already-correct `TriggerBuilder` create path), extracted as a testable `ShiftDayName` with +1/−1/wraparound unit cases. Re-verified end-to-end: republished the agent, re-synced, and `Weekly-Analysis` now reads `45 6 * * 1`, consistent with its Monday next-run; the same-day (non-crossing) weeklies were unaffected. Agent 52 tests (+6). *(2026-07-09)*

- **Real cron→Windows-trigger conversion** wired end-to-end (apply route → structured `trigger` → agent `TriggerBuilder`), with preview endpoints and live confidence warnings. *(2026-07-07)*
- **TaskHub-native tasks**: backend scheduler (30s tick, missed-run grace), HTTP job executor, connector, create/delete, violet identity + dashboard platform filter (design doc archived locally under `docs/archive/specs/`). *(2026-07-07)*
- **Run history & failure surfacing**: Run History tab (status, timestamp, duration, log snippet); red "Run failed" indicators on cards/list. *(2026-07-07)*
- **Stale-task pruning on sync**: tasks deleted natively no longer linger in TaskHub. *(2026-07-07)*
- **Agent reconnect resilience**: 30s watchdog survives backend restarts and boot-order races; startup task registered with no 72h execution limit and safe re-publish. *(2026-07-07)*
- **Platform selector in New Task modal**: create TaskHub-native *or* Windows tasks ad-hoc; Windows tasks land under the `\TaskHub\` scheduler folder; clone-flow trigger conversion fixed. *(2026-07-07)*
- **Task search on the dashboard**: free-text search across name/category/path/command/schedule with multi-term narrowing, match counter, `/` shortcut, Esc-to-clear; composes with the category/platform/active filters and all four views. *(2026-07-07)*
- **Standard dark/light/system theme system**: replaced the hardcoded blue-tinted palette with a neutral-surface semantic token set wired into Tailwind v4 (`@theme inline` + `.light`/`.dark` CSS-variable overrides). Added a `useTheme` hook (persists to `localStorage` `taskhub.theme`, follows the OS in *system* mode), a `ThemeToggle` (Light/Dark/System) in the sidebar, and a FOUC-preventing bootstrap in `index.html`. ~210 hardcoded `slate-*`/`blue-*`/`text-white` utility classes across 13 files migrated to `background`/`surface`/`muted`/`border`/`foreground`/`primary` tokens. Accent scheme: **violet** `primary` for primary actions (New Task, Sync Now, active tabs/chips) and **emerald** `success` for Run/execute buttons; native-task violet identity + red/green status colors retained. Neutral surfaces flip between light and dark; dark remains the default. *(2026-07-07)*
- **Settings page + non-blocking toasts + live connection health**: shipped a functional client-side **Settings** tab (was a placeholder) — Appearance (theme), Dashboard defaults (view / category+platform filters / active-only), Behavior (confirm-before-run, Local↔UTC schedule-time display), Notifications (success/failure toasts, desktop-notify-on-failure), Data (export/import/reset preferences, reset platform links), About (version, backend status, API origin, links) — all persisted via a `useSettings` hook (`localStorage` `taskhub.settings`). Replaced blocking `alert()`/`confirm()` on the run/sync paths with a lightweight `ToastProvider`/`useToast`. Added a **Connections** section + reactive sidebar status wired to live `GET /api/tasks/health` (per-platform state / reason / last-sync, "Check now" refresh), replacing the previously hardcoded "Agent Online / Claude Healthy" rows. Deleted the unused Vite-scaffold `App.tsx`/`App.css` (`main.tsx` is the real entry point). Deferred (need new backend + P0): real agent pairing and login/profile — see P0/Go-public. *(2026-07-08)*
- **Toast migration + honest Sync/Import + last-synced chip**: migrated the four remaining modals (`CreateTaskModal`, `CloneTaskModal`, `ApplyTemplateModal`, `TaskModal` delete) off blocking `alert()` onto the toast system. Split the dashboard header's single "Sync Now" (which opened the Import picker) into a real **Sync Now** (re-pulls status/schedules for already-tracked categories via `POST /tasks/sync`, with spinner) and a distinct **Import** (opens the discovery modal); added a **"synced N ago"** chip driven by the connections' `lastSync`. Remaining `confirm()` gates (run + delete) are intentional and still native. *(2026-07-08)*
- **Synced Windows schedules normalized to cron**: the agent now reads each task's first trigger (`TriggerReader`, the inverse of `TriggerBuilder`) and sends it — plus null-safe run times — in `task:full_list`; the backend converts it to a 5-field cron via the existing `convertWindowsTriggerToCron` and stores `schedule` + `nextRunTime` on the task (`TaskService.upsertTasks` refreshes them but won't wipe a good schedule on a failed conversion). Fixes the "No direct schedule" and "1/1/1" (`DateTime.MinValue`/1899 sentinel) lies in the Schedule view. Verified via agent xUnit (`TriggerReaderTests`, incl. a Builder↔Reader round-trip) + backend Vitest; a live Windows-agent smoke test is still pending (see Dogfood guardrail). *(2026-07-08)*
- **Full task detail view + faceted filter chips**: the task modal's Overview (`TaskModal.tsx`) no longer dumps raw JSON — it parses the synced metadata into a summary grid (Status / Last Result / Next Run / Last Run) plus **Schedule** (human-readable + UTC cron chip, or an honest "no direct schedule" note), **Action** (native HTTP job, Windows exec actions, or a plain command — with an honest "agent didn't report this" fallback), and **Settings** (scheduler state, enabled, run-as, run level, logon type, author, description — section hides when the agent reports none); raw metadata moved into a collapsible `<details>`. New `describeCron` util (`utils/schedule.ts`, unit-tested) returns null rather than guessing. The **agent** now reports the missing pieces: `AgentTaskInfo` + `Win32TaskScheduler` read each task's actions (exec path/args/working dir) and principal/registration settings (enabled, description, author, userId, runLevel, logonType) under one defensive guard, emitted camelCase in `task:full_list` (flows through `WindowsAgentConnector`'s `metadata: t` with no backend change). Separately, the Dashboard's category **and** platform chips are now faceted: each reflects the active-only + other-dimension filters so empty tags drop out (counts too), with the selected chip pinned. Verified live (Windows + native tasks) + agent build/tests (22) + frontend tests (40, incl. new `schedule` suite). *(2026-07-08)*
- **Template library filters, search & grouping**: the Templates page (`TemplatesScreen`) went from a flat card dump to a filterable catalog — free-text **search** (name / description / command / category / script type / target platforms, multi-term), a **Type** toggle (All / Starters / Patterns), and faceted **OS** + **Tags (category)** chip rows with counts that reflect each other's selection (empty combinations drop out, whole rows collapse when only one value remains). Results are grouped into **Starters** (parameterized) and **Use-case patterns** sections with per-group counts; cards gained a "Starter" badge. Added a distinct no-match empty state and Clear-filters/clear-search affordances. Extracted a reusable `TemplateCard`; label helpers map the enum-ish `os`/`category`/`scriptType` values to human strings. Verified live (facet interaction, search narrowing, clear). *(2026-07-08)*
- Docs restructure: post-reorg links fixed, .NET 10 alignment, contracts kept in sync with implementation.
- **README visual overhaul**: Command Grid banner logo wired into the hero (`images/TaskHub-Images/`), real app screenshots captured from the current build (`images/screenshots/` — dashboard, templates, task detail, light theme), quick-nav link row (docs / issues), numbered Quick Start with back-to-top links, collapsible screenshots section; docs hub gained a numbered "Start here" path. Follow-up: 5 more screenshots (list/kanban/schedule views, New Task + Apply Template modals) in a collapsed 2-column gallery, install paths split into standalone guides (`docs/install/guides/` — Windows / macOS / clone), user guides relocated to `docs/user-guides/guides/`, and a key-guides table on the root README. *(2026-07-08)*

#### ✅ P0 — Security hardening (blockers before hosting the backend) — COMPLETE

All P0 blockers shipped 2026-07-09 (see Completed › Security hardening for each). The backend is no longer gated on security for a *single-user* host; a real multi-user launch still needs the account system + operations items in the Go-public checklist. Original gate (analysis §4): nothing shipped to a public host until these were done.

- [x] **WebSocket auth** *(shipped 2026-07-09 — see Completed)*: pairing-secret HMAC handshake (`io.use` middleware), per-session HMAC signing on `task:run`/`task:create`/`task:set_status` with the agent refusing unverifiable commands, unauthenticated sockets rejected, Socket.io CORS restricted to `ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, agent server URL + secret moved to env (WSS-capable), fail-fast on missing `AGENT_PAIRING_SECRET`. *(Multi-agent/per-user pairing secret still uses the shared-secret MVP — see the per-user pairing flow in Go-public.)*
- [x] **Encrypt `PlatformConnection.config` at rest** *(shipped 2026-07-09 — see Completed)*: the AES-256-GCM helpers are now wired into every config read/write via a single `connectionConfig` module; new writes are encrypted, legacy plaintext rows still read (migration-free).
- [x] **Multi-tenancy basics** *(shipped 2026-07-09 — see Completed)*: every task route now derives `userId` from the JWT (`req.user.id`) instead of a hardcoded placeholder, and all by-id lookups (list/patch/delete/run/executions) are scoped so one user can't read/mutate/run another's tasks (IDOR closed). Backend fails fast on a missing/weak `JWT_SECRET`; the committed dev JWT was removed from `frontend/src/api.ts` (now env-sourced) and `JWT_SECRET` rotated (invalidating the leaked token). *(Templates remain a shared seeded catalog by design; the git-history secret scrub is tracked under Go-public.)*
- [x] **Command handling** *(shipped 2026-07-09 — see Completed)*: the agent no longer wraps commands in `cmd.exe /c`; the backend structures the resolved command into `{executable, args[]}` (server-side, `utils/commandParser.ts`), the agent registers a direct ExecAction with Windows per-arg quoting (`ArgumentQuoting.cs`), and the action is covered by the task:create HMAC signature. *(Residual: robust per-parameter server-side substitution from raw params — see P1 command-handling follow-up.)*
- [x] Agent config file/env for server URL + WSS support *(shipped 2026-07-09 with the WebSocket-auth work — see Completed)*: `TASKHUB_SERVER_URL` (WSS-capable) resolves from env or the gitignored `appsettings.json`; no longer hardcoded to `http://localhost:3000`.

#### 🟠 P1 — Correctness & honesty

- [x] **Multi-day weekly schedules no longer silently collapse to one day** *(fixed 2026-07-14, found while validating the MCP server)*: `convertCronToWindowsTrigger` ran `parseInt` over the whole day-of-week field, and `parseInt('1-5')` is `1` — so `0 9 * * 1-5` ("every weekday") converted to a **Monday-only** trigger reporting **confidence 1.0 with no warnings**, as did any list (`1,3,5`) or range. The same truncation ran in all three layers: the reverse path (`convertWindowsTriggerToCron`) kept `daysOfWeek[0]`, so a real Mon/Wed/Fri Windows task **imported** as Mondays-only; and the agent's `TriggerBuilder` resolved only `DaysOfWeek[0]`, so it would have **registered** Mondays-only even once the server sent all five. Fixed in all three with a real day parser (lists, ranges, `0`/`7` → one Sunday, unparseable → warned fallback instead of a guessed day), plus a lossy-conversion warning for minute/hour steps that don't divide 60/24 evenly (`*/7` now scores 0.7 and says why; `*/15` still 1.0). 9 new tests (6 TS + 3 C#), each failing before the fix. **Needs an agent republish** to take effect on the create path (see [troubleshooting #7](troubleshooting/README.md#7-new-agent-command-502-times-out-until-the-agent-is-republished)) — the backend/import halves are live.
- [x] **Live-agent smoke test for schedule normalization** *(done 2026-07-09 — see Completed)*: dogfooded against a real Windows Task Scheduler sync (282 tasks). Real Daily/Weekly triggers normalize to correct 5-field UTC crons + real next-run times; non-cron triggers (boot/logon/unlock) honestly show no schedule. **Found & fixed a real bug**: `TriggerReader` converted the start time to UTC but read the day-of-week without the matching roll, so a weekly trigger crossing midnight in UTC reported the wrong day (e.g. Sun 23:45 PDT showed `* * 0` instead of `* * 1`). Fixed + unit-tested + re-verified live.
- [ ] **System-status honesty** *(mostly shipped 2026-07-08)*: sidebar + Settings > Connections show live per-platform health from `GET /api/tasks/health` (no longer hardcoded, polled every 45s); a "synced N ago" chip and honest Sync/Import split have shipped. The 45s poll is now backed by push too — the browser live-updates channel (see Completed) invalidates `['connections']` on `task:updated`.
- [x] **Browser live updates** *(shipped 2026-07-09 — see Completed)*: the dashboard opens a receive-only Socket.IO connection to a JWT-authed `/ui` namespace and invalidates `['tasks']`/`['connections']` on `task:updated`, which the backend emits (per-user room) on every task mutation + native scheduler fire. Replaces polling with push.
- [x] **Local-time schedule display in the task detail view** *(shipped 2026-07-09 — see Completed)*: `describeCron` now takes the Settings Local/UTC mode; in local mode it converts the recurring clock time to the viewer's zone and rolls the day-of-week across midnight (via a `now`-anchored reference instant, so it respects the current DST offset). Default is local. The same helper is ready to power the P2 Apply-modal schedule preview.
- [x] **Filter chips reflect the active view** *(shipped 2026-07-08 — see Completed)*: Dashboard category and platform chips are now faceted from the active-only + other-dimension filters, so empty tags drop out (counts too) and the selected chip stays pinned.
- [x] **Agent-auth hardening follow-ups** *(shipped 2026-07-10 — see Completed)*: `task:create`'s `trigger` is now signed into the HMAC (canonical cross-language trigger serialization pinned by the golden vector), the agent rejects a replayed `(ts, sig)` within the freshness window, and the agent keeps the prior session key valid through a reconnect overlap so an in-flight command isn't false-rejected. Verified live end-to-end incl. a >120s-old connection.
- [x] **Command-handling follow-up** *(shipped 2026-07-10 — see Completed)*: the Apply flow now sends raw parameter values and the backend owns `{{placeholder}}` substitution per-token (`utils/templateCommand.ts`), so a parameter is always exactly one argument regardless of quotes/spaces in its value; parameters are validated server-side against the template's declared definitions. Verified live: a hostile `scriptPath` with an unbalanced quote registers as a single escaped argument in Task Scheduler.
- [x] **Backend hygiene** *(shipped 2026-07-10 — see Completed)*: Zod validation at route boundaries + a single error middleware, shared Prisma singleton (`src/db.ts`, was 9 separate clients), and batched sync upserts (chunked `$transaction`, ~3 round trips instead of 284 for a full Windows sync).
- [x] **Remaining QA** (from archived Phase 4; completed 2026-07-10): integration suite against real Postgres, Playwright E2E with a deterministic mock agent, stale-pruning resilience/soak work, security audit, dependency remediation, and performance baseline are all shipped (see Completed). The audit intentionally leaves public-hosting risks in the Go-public checklist rather than treating them as local-MVP blockers: login/API rate limiting, REST CORS restriction, Helmet/security headers, real account/session flow, per-user agent pairing, and SSRF guardrails for hosted native HTTP jobs.

- [x] **`scripts/taskhub.ps1 status` reports services down while they are serving** *(logged 2026-07-28 from the analysis; **fixed 2026-07-28** — see CHANGELOG › Fixed and [troubleshooting #23a](troubleshooting/README.md#23a-and-the-same-probe-reported-four-services-down-while-all-four-were-serving))*: shipped as scoped. Every service is now probed by asking the service — `GET /api/health`, an HTTP `GET /`, the docker healthcheck / `pg_isready`, and a **RESP `PING` spoken directly over the socket** for Redis (chosen over `docker exec redis-cli` so the probe survives the broken docker CLI that helped cause the false negative). The port is corroboration only, and `Test-PortBound` attempts a real TCP connect before falling back to `Get-NetTCPConnection`, whose CIM provider was the missing piece. **The design point the logged item didn't anticipate: two states weren't enough.** Both probes had been wrapped in `catch { $false }`, so *"I could not run the probe"* and *"the service is down"* printed as the same word — so a third state, **`WARN`**, now covers present-but-unconfirmable, and every row prints **the signal it used** rather than a bare verdict. The `up` liveness gate reads the same probe (a held-but-dead `:3000` names the holder instead of declaring the backend up), and `Stop-Port` no longer claims "already stopped" for a port a container proxy still answers. Verified in both directions — a simulated broken environment reproducing the reviewer's conditions, and a raw TCP listener that accepts and never speaks HTTP — plus parse checks under pwsh 7 **and** Windows PowerShell 5.1 with a non-ASCII byte scan (the 5.1 em-dash trap). *(Original finding below.)*

  *(Original)* the reviewer's `status` run called Postgres, Redis, backend `:3000`, and frontend `:7373` all down, **while `/api/health` returned 200 and the frontend returned 200**; `Get-NetTCPConnection` returned no rows for ports that were demonstrably serving HTTP. This is the **exact inverse** of the failure that produced [troubleshooting #23](troubleshooting/README.md#23-network-error-after-a-reboot--the-database-system-is-starting-up) — there, `Test-Port 3000` said *"backend already up"* about a dead container's held port and refused to start the real one. Both directions come from the same root: **the script probes ports, and a port is not a service.** The generalization is already in the traps table (*never probe your own service with a bare port check*), but the script itself was never changed to follow it. Fix: make `status` (and the liveness gate `Test-Port` feeds) prefer a **service-specific probe** — `GET /api/health` for the backend, an HTTP `GET /` for the frontend, `pg_isready` / `docker compose ps --format json` + healthcheck state for the data services — and fall back to the port check only as corroboration, reporting *which* signal it used. A control surface that is wrong in both directions is worse than no control surface, because it is consulted first and believed.

- [x] **Dark-by-default is documented but not implemented** *(logged 2026-07-28 from the analysis; **fixed 2026-07-28** — see CHANGELOG › Changed)*: resolved in favor of **dark**, as recommended: `readStored()` now falls back to `'dark'`, and `system` stays available as a mode the user picks on purpose. The pre-paint script in `index.html` — which had *always* claimed in its comment to default to dark while actually passing `'system'` — was corrected too, and a test pins the two defaults together so they cannot drift again. All six records that already said "dark by default" (CLAUDE.md §9, `index.css`, README badge + feature table, `SKILL.md`, the functional-test checklist) are now true. Verified in a real browser via the theme toggle's `aria-pressed`, which names the *mode* and is therefore discriminating on a machine whose OS already prefers dark — `.dark` on `<html>` alone would not have been. The corresponding Open decision below is closed. *(Original finding below.)*

  *(Original)* `frontend/src/hooks/useTheme.ts:15-17` returns **`'system'`** both when there is no stored `taskhub.theme` and when the stored value is unrecognized — so a fresh install follows the OS, and the reviewer's browser pass rendered the app **light**. Meanwhile `index.css` comments, `CLAUDE.md` §9 (*"Dark theme is the **default**, not an opt-in. The light variant is the toggle."*), and the README all state dark is the default. Nothing is broken; the **records are lying**, which is the §11a failure mode aimed at a user this time — someone reads "dark by default", sees light, and concludes their install is wrong. Pick one and make all four agree: either `readStored()` falls back to `'dark'` (keeping `system` as an explicit user choice), or `system` is the intended default and CLAUDE.md §9 + the CSS comments + the README change to say so. **Recommend defaulting to `dark`** — the design system, the token set, and both public sites are built dark-first, so "follow the OS" ships a first paint the product was not designed in. Tracked as an Open decision below since it changes a stated product convention.

- [x] **The closed mobile sidebar is still reachable by keyboard and screen reader** *(logged 2026-07-28 from the analysis; **fixed 2026-07-28** — see CHANGELOG › Fixed)*: the `<aside>` is now `inert` + `aria-hidden` while closed and **only below `md`**, via a new `useMediaQuery` hook — a Tailwind class can only change how something looks, so the breakpoint had to be readable in JS to make the decision at all. Tests assert the **behavior** (`byRole` sees only accessible elements) rather than the attribute, and are mutation-checked. **Honest verification note:** the browser window would not resize in this environment (it reports success while `innerWidth` stays 2124 — the same limitation recorded against the gallery work), so the `<375px` layout was not re-driven live; instead the real browser confirmed the one thing jsdom structurally cannot — that Chrome refuses `focus()` on the element once `inert` is applied, dropping focus to `<body>`. Worth a real device check alongside the other deferred mobile pass. *(Original finding below.)*

  *(Original)* `frontend/src/components/Sidebar.tsx:68` hides the drawer with `-translate-x-full`, which is a **visual** transform only — the nav buttons stay in the DOM, in the tab order, and in role queries, so a keyboard user tabbing through the dashboard walks an invisible menu before reaching the page. The backdrop at `:63` carries `aria-hidden`, but the `<aside>` it covers does not. Fix: mark the aside `inert` + `aria-hidden` while `open === false` **on mobile only** (the `md:` breakpoint renders it statically and it must stay reachable there), or conditionally render below the breakpoint. This lands under P1 rather than polish because the mobile drawer shipped specifically to meet the `<375px` first-class-mobile requirement (P3 frontend refactor, slice 3) and a drawer that traps focus behind itself does not meet it.

#### 🟡 P2 — Product value

- [x] **A "Tools" tab — bulk export/backup + AI-tool connection downloads** *(logged 2026-07-28; **shipped 2026-07-28** — see CHANGELOG › Added)*: shipped as scoped, with the design points below all honored. **What the build settled:**
  - **No agent protocol change was needed, as predicted** — `task:export` already accepted an arbitrary `taskPath`, so `POST /api/tools/export/tasks` is backend + frontend only, and **no agent republish**. A batched `task:exportBatch` verb was not needed either: bounded concurrency (4 in flight) exported **95 tasks in 1.7s**.
  - **Verified from outside TaskHub**, per the rule that the reporting layer can't be its own witness: a bulk-exported XML fed back through Windows' own `Schedule.Service` COM parser was accepted, action and trigger read back intact; and it is **byte-identical** (3,908 bytes, zero differing bytes) to the same task from `GET /api/tasks/:id/export` on both the ZIP and base64 paths. All 95 files carry the `FF FE` BOM; no `Microsoft/` folder in the archive.
  - **New routes live under `/api/tools`, not `/api/tasks`** — every route on `tasks.ts` competes with `/:id` in Express's declaration-order matching (the trap that forced `DELETE /tasks/missing` above `DELETE /tasks/:id`), and a bulk export has no task id to be confused with.
  - **The connect pack is newly authored content, not the repo skill** — confirmed as the right call: an audience test now asserts no repo internals (`catalogSync`, `normalize.ts`, `bundled.ts`, `registry:build`) and no repo-relative links reach the shipped pack. It compiles into a bundled module via `npm run connectpack:build`, guarded by a drift test, because `tsc` emits only `.js` and loose `.md` under `src/` would never reach `dist/` — a fourth thing that would run stale, and the worst kind, since dev under `tsx` would have looked fine.
  - **Honest residual:** the native folder picker dialog cannot be driven by automation, so it stays a manual check; the writing logic behind it is unit-tested against a fake directory handle. And the picker's transient-activation requirement is now [troubleshooting #24](troubleshooting/README.md#24-showdirectorypicker-throws-must-be-handling-a-user-gesture-after-an-await).
  - **Restore is still phase 2** and deliberately unshipped — export alone is half a backup. The UTF-16 output is restore-ready by construction (`Register-ScheduledTask -Xml`), and the task-authoring reference documents the re-import command, but TaskHub has no restore UI. Tracked as its own item below.

  *(Original scoping notes retained below.)* A **5th tab** alongside Dashboard / Templates / Platforms / Settings (`frontend/src/components/Sidebar.tsx:28`), housing utilities that aren't per-task actions. Two tools at launch. *(Naming caveat: "Tools" is a junk-drawer label — it earns its place only while things genuinely land there. If a sharper name emerges once the second and third tools exist, rename it then rather than accumulating miscellany.)*

  ### Tool 1 — Mass export / backup of Windows Task Scheduler tasks

  Export **all** tasks, or **all tasks in one folder**, as native Task Scheduler XML, to a location the user picks. Per-task export already exists (`GET /api/tasks/:id/export`, MCP `export_task`); this is the bulk case, and it is the **missing half of a disaster-recovery story we already know we need**: the bulk-clear item below exists because *one* deleted Task Scheduler folder can flag **56 tasks MISSING at once**. Today that is an unrecoverable loss — TaskHub can tell you the tasks are gone and let you clear the rows, but it cannot give you the tasks back.

  **The load-bearing finding — this needs no agent protocol change.** `task:export` (`agent/TaskHub.Agent/AgentService.cs:288`) takes a raw `taskPath` and calls `_scheduler.ExportTaskXml(taskPath)`; it is **read-only and unsigned** (like `task:list`) and it does **not** check that the path belongs to a TaskHub-tracked task. `WindowsAgentConnector.exportTask` (`backend/src/connectors/WindowsAgentConnector.ts:166`) passes `externalId` straight through as `taskPath`. So the agent can already export **any** task on the machine — the restriction to tracked tasks lives entirely in the backend route, which is keyed on a DB row id. Combined with `/discover`, which already enumerates every task with its path, "export everything in every folder" is **backend + frontend work only**. A batched `task:exportBatch` verb is an *optimization* (see the timeout note below), not a prerequisite — and optimizations don't need an agent republish.

  Design points, each of which has already bitten somewhere in this repo:
  - **Exclude `\Microsoft\` by default.** On a real machine the split is **95 user tasks across 13 folders vs. 257 `\Microsoft\` system tasks** (measured during the untracked-tasks work). Dumping 352 files by default buries the 95 that matter. Same call as that item: surface the system count, don't fold it into the total.
  - **Decide tracked-only vs. whole-machine, and say which.** Tracked-only reuses the existing route per task. Whole-machine (what was asked for) exports tasks TaskHub has no row for — strictly more useful as a backup, and now known to be cheap. Whichever ships, the UI must state which it did; "Export all" meaning "all the ones we happen to have imported" is exactly the invisible-fence problem the untracked-tasks item was filed for.
  - **Zip on the server, not in the browser.** Task Scheduler XML is delivered **UTF-16 LE + BOM** because that is the only encoding Windows re-imports. Shipping those bytes to the frontend as JSON strings and zipping client-side walks straight into the trap MCP `export_task` already hit, where axios decoded UTF-16 as UTF-8 into mojibake and needed a raw-bytes client path. Build the archive server-side where the bytes are still bytes.
  - **The 15s timeout is per-call** (`WindowsAgentConnector.ts:194`). 95 sequential round-trips is a long silent wait and 352 is worse; needs concurrency or a batch verb, **plus a progress signal** — the `task:exported` handler already correlates by `taskExternalId`, so concurrent in-flight exports work without protocol changes.
  - **"Browse for a location" has a hard browser limit.** A web page cannot write to an arbitrary filesystem path. The honest implementation is the **File System Access API** (`showDirectoryPicker()`) — a real folder picker, Chromium-only but `localhost` is a secure context, so it works for a local-first app — with a **single-ZIP download fallback** everywhere else.
  - **Do NOT solve the picker by adding an agent file-write verb.** It is the tempting shortcut (the agent is already on the machine, already elevated, and could just write where told), and it would convert a task-scheduler agent into a **general elevated arbitrary-file-write primitive** reachable from the backend. That is a categorically larger attack surface than everything the agent does today, traded for a directory picker. The browser limitation is worth living with.
  - **Exported XML contains full command lines, arguments, and the principal each task runs as.** One task at a time is one thing; a whole-machine dump into a folder is a different exposure — say so at the point of export rather than silently writing 95 files that may contain API keys in `argv`.

  **Export is half a backup — restore is the natural phase 2** and should be named now so it isn't discovered later: Windows re-imports via `schtasks /create /xml` / `Register-ScheduledTask`, and the UTF-16 output is already restore-ready by construction. Not in this slice; do not let the slice pretend to be a backup product without it. *(Now tracked separately — see **Restore tasks from an export** below.)*

  ### Tool 2 — Downloads for connecting *other* AI tools

  For users driving TaskHub from a CLI or agent this repo has no first-class instructions for: download the TaskHub **skill**, plus sibling artifacts (a system-prompt / `AGENTS.md`-style single file for hosts with no skill concept, an `.mcp.json` wiring snippet, per-host install notes) so they can teach their own tool to use TaskHub.

  **The catch that decides the work: the shipped skill is the wrong document for this audience.** `skills/taskhub/` teaches an agent to **work on the TaskHub codebase** — CLAUDE.md §6 calls it "the mirror image of `mcp-server/`". An end user with Codex, Cursor, or some other CLI wants to **use** a running TaskHub; handing them the repo skill ships them `catalogSync`, `normalize.ts`, registry publishing, and the repo's internal traps — noise at best. The user-facing pack must be **authored**, deriving from [`skills/cronsole/references/task-authoring.md`](../skills/cronsole/references/task-authoring.md) (already the closest thing to a usage guide), the MCP tool table, and the §9 invariants. **This tool is a content deliverable with a download button attached, not a download button.**

  Two more constraints:
